# Encyclopedia — Appellations

Full per-appellation entries with grape composition, aging windows, and named producers where the parser captured them. Duplicates collapsed by `name + classification`, keeping the richest copy.

## FRANCE

### BORDEAUX

#### BORDEAUX AOC

As with any large, and thus variable, appellation, the generic Bordeaux AOC is responsible for the good, bad, and ugly wines of the region.

- **Red style:** Most are simply dry, luncheon claret styles, made for early drinking and usually softened by a high Merlot content. Carmenère, Merlot, Malbec, Petit Verdot
- **White style:** All medium-dry, basic white bordeaux contain at least 4 grams of residual sugar per liter, and have a certain sweetness. It is by far the most variable appellation category, with many dull wines. If the wine contains less than 4 grams of residual sugar per liter, the Bordeaux…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,; Sémillon, Sauvignon, Muscadelle plus up to
- **Aging:** 1–5 years; 1–2 years; Immediately

#### BORDEAUX CLAIRET AOC

There are more than 50 appellations in Bordeaux and every one has its generic wines, produced by a négociant or local coopérative.


#### BORDEAUX ROSÉ AOC

The theory is that this appellation is reserved for wine deliberately produced as rosé, while “Bordeaux Clairet” is for a light-colored red wine.

- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,
- **Aging:** 1–2 years

#### BORDEAUX SEC AOC

White bordeaux with less than 4 grams of residual sugar per liter (see Bordeaux AOC).


#### BORDEAUX SUPÉRIEUR AOC

Technically superior to Bordeaux AOC by only half a degree of alcohol, yet most of these wines do seem to have a greater consistency of quality, and, therefore, value.

- **Red style:** These dry, light-bodied or medium- to fullbodied wines vary a lot but are generally fuller and richer than most red wines using the basic Bordeaux appellation. Carmenère, Merlot, Malbec, Petit Verdot
- **White style:** Dry or sometimes sweet, light- to medium-bodied white wines that are little seen. 30% in total Merlot Blanc, Colombard, Mauzac, Ondenc, Ugni Blanc; the proportion of Merlot Blanc must not exceed 15% BORDEAUX SUPÉRIEUR
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,; Sémillon, Sauvignon, Muscadelle plus up to
- **Aging:** 2–6 years; 1–2 years

#### CADILLAC AOC

Of the trio of sweet-wine areas on the right bank of the Garonne, Cadillac is the least known.

- **White style:** Attractive honey-gold wines with fresh, floral aromas and a semisweet, or sweet, fruity flavor.
- **Grapes:** Sémillon, Sauvignon Blanc, Muscadelle
- **Aging:** 3–8 years

#### CLAIRET AOC

This is a little-seen appellation: the wines are sold either as “Bordeaux Supérieur” or “Bordeaux Clairet.” This was introduced in 1990 to replace the old Bordeaux Mousseux AOC (which was phased out on December 31, 1995).

- **Sparkling style:** ROSÉ The authorities should have taken advantage of the introduction of a new appellation to allow the inclusion of white grapes for this style, as this would potentially have improved the quality. Carmenère, Merlot, Malbec, Petit Verdot THE WINE PRODUCERS OF BORDEAUX AND…
- **Grapes:** Sémillon, Sauvignon, Muscadelle, Ugni; Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,
- **Aging:** 1–2 years; 2–3 years

#### CÔTES-DEBORDEAUX-ST.-MACAIRE AOC

Situated above the Premières Côtes, opposite Cérons, this area corresponds to the ancient and tiny county of Benauge.

- **White style:** Medium-bodied, medium-sweet, or sweet wines that are attractive in a fruity way, but unpretentious.
- **Grapes:** Sémillon, Sauvignon Blanc, Muscadelle
- **Aging:** 1–3 years for dry and medium-sweet wines;; 1–3 years

#### ENTRE-DEUX-MERS AOC

This is the largest district in the region, and after the generic Bordeaux Blanc, it is its greatest volume white-wine appellation.

- **White style:** Crisp, dry, light-bodied wines that are fragrant, aromatic, and usually predominantly Sauvignon Blanc. These are clean, cool-fermented wines. They may be sold from December 1 following the harvest without any mention of primeur or nouveau. Blanc, and Muscadelle, plus a maximum…
- **Grapes:** At least 70 percent Sémillon, Sauvignon
- **Aging:** 1–2 years

#### ENTRE-DEUX-MERS-HAUT-BENAUGE AOC

These wines are drier than those of the Bordeaux-Haut-Benauge appellation, and their blends may include a greater number of grape varieties, although the same nine communes comprise both appellations.

- **White style:** These dry wines are very similar to those of Entre-Deux-Mers. Blanc, and Muscadelle, plus a maximum of 30 percent Merlot Blanc and up to 10 percent in total of Colombard, Mauzac, and Ugni Blanc
- **Grapes:** At least 70 percent Sémillon, Sauvignon
- **Aging:** 1–3 years

#### GRAVES DE VAYRES AOC

An enclave of gravelly soil on the left bank of the Dordogne, this appellation produces a substantial quantity of excellent-value red and white wines.

- **Red style:** These are well-colored, aromatic, mediumbodied wines with fragrant, juicy-spicy, predominantly Merlot fruit. They are richer than those found elsewhere in Entre-Deux-Mers. Carmenère, Merlot, Malbec, Petit Verdot
- **White style:** Mostly dry and off-dry styles of fresh, fragrant, and fruity wines made for early drinking. Occasionally, sweeter styles are made. These wines may be sold from December 1 following the harvest without any mention of primeur or nouveau. plus a maximum of 30 percent Merlot Blanc
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,; Sémillon, Sauvignon Blanc, and Muscadelle
- **Aging:** 4–10 years; 1–3 years

#### HAUT-BENAUGE AOC


#### ROSÉ AOC

This appellation has a small cast—and Château Lascombe’s Rosé de Lascombes still tops the bill.

- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,
- **Aging:** 1–2 years

### MÉDOC

#### HAUT-MÉDOC AOC

This AOC encompasses the Médoc’s four finest communes—Margaux, St.-Julien, Pauillac, and St.-Estèphe—as well as the less well-known Listrac and Moulis communes.

- **Red style:** These dry wines have a generosity of fruit tempered by a firm structure, and are mediumto full-bodied. Malbec, Petit Verdot, Carmenère 5–8 years (others)
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc, Merlot,
- **Aging:** 6–15 years (crus classés);

#### LE MEYNIEU AOC

Haut-Médoc Cru Bourgeois cV This property is under the same ownership as Château Lavillotte, which is situated in St.-Estèphe.

- **Red style:** This wine can be dark and tannic, but as from the 1996 vintage it has displayed more fruit and finesse. Merlot 45%, Petit Verdot 10%
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon 62%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 60%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 55%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 65%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 70%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 57%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 45%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 50%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 54%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 20%,; Merlot 50%, Cabernet; Merlot 50%, Cabernet Sauvignon; Cabernet Sauvignon 44%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 53%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 40%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 42%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 58%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 67%,; Cabernet Sauvignon 72%,

#### LISTRAC-MÉDOC AOC

Significant funds have been invested in a number of high-performance châteaux in this commune, although its heavy clay soil does not have anything like as much potential as the gravel ridges found in the most famous Médoc appellations.

- **Red style:** These dry, medium- to full-bodied wines have the fruit and finesse of St.-Julien combined with the firmness of St.-Estèphe. The most successful wines tend to have a large proportion of Merlot, which enjoys the HautMédoc’s clay soil. Carmenère, Merlot, Malbec, Petit Verdot
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,
- **Aging:** 5–10 years

#### MARGAUX AOC

lowest yield per acre of the four famous Médoc AOCs.

- **Red style:** Exquisite, dry, medium-bodied, and sometimes full-bodied, wines that can be deepcolored and fabulously rich, yet they have great finesse and a silky finish. Carmenère, Merlot, Malbec, Petit Verdot 5–10 years (others) brings power and style together to produce wines of…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,
- **Aging:** 5–20 years (crus classés);; 9–25 years (crus classés);

#### MÉDOC AOC

Technically, this appellation covers the entire Merlot 35%, Petit Verdot 5% Médoc, but most wines actually come from north of the Haut-Médoc in the area which was Second wine: Château Peyvigneau formerly called the Bas-Médoc.

- **Red style:** Dark-coloured, plummy wine, Situated on a good gravel bank although the style is less sophisticated. with a lot of soft, ripe fruit. behind Château Lagrange, the wine,
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon 60%,
- **Aging:** 8–20 years

#### MOULIS AOC

- **Red style:** These wines are very stylish, blackcurrant fruit and ripe acidity, OR MOULIS-ENMédoc appellations. with lush Cabernet fruit backed up bourgeois. The area under vine is slightly less with much more supple tannin

#### MOULIS-EN-MÉDOC AOC

One of the two communal appellations located on the Atlantic side of the Médoc, Moulis-en-Médoc is smaller and potentially more interesting than its neighbor Listrac.

- **Red style:** These dry, medium-bodied, sometimes full-bodied, wines have more power than those of Margaux, but far less finesse. Carmenère, Merlot, Malbec, Petit Verdot
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,
- **Aging:** 5–12 years

#### PAUILLAC AOC

The potential of St-Estèphe is exemplified by Cos d’Estournel, which is one of the best deuxièmes crus in the Médoc, but the strength of this appellation lies in its range of crus bourgeois.

- **Red style:** If Pauillac is the stallion of the four famous appellations, St.-Estèphe must be the dray horse. These dry, full-bodied wines are big and strong, yet not without dignity. St-Estèphe demands affection and, with the rich fruit of a sunny year, deserves it. These most enjoyable,…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,
- **Aging:** 8–25 years (crus classés);

#### ST-ESTÈPHE AOC

months, with 70 per cent new oak.

- **Red style:** These vividly coloured wines have a fine bouquet and firm tannic structure.

#### ST-JULIEN AOC

The best Margaux are potentially the greatest wines in the whole of Bordeaux, but this is an appellation that covers five communes encompassing a great diversity of soil and some of its wines not unnaturally have a tendency to disappoint.

- **Red style:** These are dry, medium-bodied, sometimes full-bodied, wines that have purity of style, varietal flavor, and can be longlived. Well balanced and elegant, these wines fall somewhere between the lushness that is typical of Margaux and the firmer structure of Pauillac. Carmenère,…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,
- **Aging:** 6–20 years (crus classés);

### (General)

#### ALOXE-CORTON AOC

This village is more Côte de Nuits than Côte de Beaune, as its 99 percent red-wine production suggests.

- **Red style:** These deeply colored, firm-structured wines with compact fruit are reminiscent of reds from northern Côte de Nuits. They are excellent value for money.
- **White style:** Very little Aloxe-Corton blanc is made, but Daniel Senard makes a lovely buttery-rich, concentrated pure Pinot Gris wine (which, although it is definitely a white wine, makes it red according to the regulations!). • Daniel Senard • Tollot-Beaut ALOXE-CORTON
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 10–20 years; 4–8 years
- **Producers:** Edmond Cornu & Fils, P. DubreuilFontaine, Follin-Arbelet, Maurice Martray

#### ALSACE AOC

This appellation covers all the wines of Alsace (with the exception of Alsace Grand Cru and Crémant d’Alsace), but 95 percent of the wines are often sold according to grape variety.


#### ALSACE GRAND CRU AOC

The current production of grand cru wine is approximately 4 percent of the total volume of AOC Alsace. Because every cru, or growth, makes a wine of a specific character, it is impossible to give a generalized description.

- **White style:** See The Grands Crus of Alsace, p.187 Gris, Sylvaner (Zotzenberg only) ALSACE SÉLECTION DE
- **Grapes:** Muscat, Riesling, Gewurztraminer, Pinot

#### AND DU FEL VDQS

Situated south of Cahors, this VDQS is a northern extension of the Frontonnais, with vines growing on a boulbènes soil that is similar to that of Bordeaux’s Entre-Deux-Mers district.

- **Red style:** Light, rustic wines that are best consumed locally because they need all the local ambience they can get. Gamay, Jurançon Noir, Merlot, Mouyssaguès, Négrette, Pinot Noir
- **White style:** Light, dry, and crisp wines for unpretentious quaffing. ROSÉ Light, fresh, and dry rosés. Gamay, Jurançon Noir, Merlot, Mouyssaguès, Négrette, Pinot Noir VINS
- **Grapes:** A minimum of 80% (in total) of Mauzac,; Mauzac, Sauvignon Blanc, Sémillon,; Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon, Fer,; Chenin Blanc, Mauzac
- **Aging:** 3–6 years; 1–3 years; 1–2 years; Upon purchase
- **Producers:** C. V. de Lavilledieu du Temple, Jean-Marc Viguer

#### ANJOU AOC

The Anjou district encompasses the vineyards of Saumur; thus Saumur may be sold as Anjou, but not vice versa. The red wines are by far the best, the whites the worst, and the rosé wines, although waning in popularity, remain the most famous.

- **Red style:** Dry, medium- to full-bodied wines, made mostly from pure Cabernet Franc or with a touch of Cabernet Sauvignon. These delightful wines are best drunk young, although the odd oakaged wine of surprising complexity can be found. Pineau d’Aunis Pareils) • Château de Fesles • Richard…
- **White style:** Although these wines vary from dry to sweet and from light- to full-bodied types, there are too many aggressively acid-dry or simply mediocre medium-sweet Chenin Blanc wines in the appellation. Some improvement has been made by growers maximizing the 20 percent Chardonnay and…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon,; A minimum of 80% Chenin Blanc and a; Predominantly Grolleau, with varying
- **Aging:** 1–3 years; Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Des Baumard, Bouvet-Ladubay (Non, Philippe Delesvaux, Les Grandes Vignes, C. V. de la Loire (Elysis), Château La Varière

#### ANJOU GAMAY AOC

Gamay is only allowed in Anjou AOC wines if the name of the grape is added to the appellation on the label.

- **Red style:** Dry to medium-dry, light-bodied wines that are rarely of great interest. These wines may be sold as primeur or nouveau as from the third Thursday of November following the harvest.
- **Grapes:** Gamay
- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** De la Charmoise, Roy René (La Creusette)

#### ANJOU MOUSSEUX AOC

This traditional method wine is softer, but less popular than its Saumur equivalent, although it may come from the communes within Saumur itself.

- **Sparkling style:** ROSÉ If you want to know what Anjou Rosé tastes like with bubbles, try this light- to medium-bodied wine, which is mostly sold as demi-sec. Malbec, Gamay, Grolleau, Pineau d’Aunis
- **Grapes:** A minimum of 60% Chenin Blanc plus; Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,
- **Aging:** 1–2 years; Upon purchase

#### ANJOU PÉTILLANT AOC

A little-used appellation for gently sparkling traditional method wines with a minimum of nine months’ bottle age, which must be sold in ordinary still-wine bottles with regular corks.

- **Grapes:** A minimum of 80% Chenin Blanc and a; Grolleau, Cabernet Franc, Cabernet
- **Aging:** Upon purchase

#### ANJOU ROSÉ AOC

See Anjou AOC


#### ANJOU-VILLAGES AOC

This superior, red-wine-only appellation was first delimited in 1986, but it did not come into effect until 1991. If you buy from the best growers, you will get some of the finest red wines that the Loire has to offer.

- **Red style:** The very best wines can be deeply colored with a creamy-raspberry aroma and flavor. Pierre Bise • Des Griottes • L. & F. Martin • De la Motte • Ogereau • De la Poterie • De Putille • Des Quattre Quarres • Michel Robineau • Des Saulaies • Château La Varière ANJOU–VILLAGES
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon
- **Aging:** 2–6 years
- **Producers:** Patrick Baudoin, De la Bergerie, Château

#### AOC OR CÔTES DU RHÔNE ST.MAURICE-SUR-EYGUES AOC

Laudun, one of the appellation’s four original villages, produces the other wine (see Côtes du Rhône Chusclan AOC) that can be made from vines grown outside the village indicated—in this case from St.-Victor-Lacoste and Tresques – and excels in making fine, fresh, and spicy red w

- **Producers:** Château St.-Maurice

#### ARBOIS AOC

This is the best-known appellation of the Jura, from in and around the town of Arbois.

- **Red style:** Trousseau wines are rich, and sometimes coarse, and Pinot wines are light and rustic.
- **White style:** Light, fresh Chardonnays are best; pure Savagnin can be delicately aromatic when not blended with declassified vin jaune, good examples of which are world renowned. ROSÉ Famous for its firm and distinctive dry rosé wines made in the pale vin gris style. Chatelain • Michel Gahir…
- **Grapes:** Trousseau, Poulsard, Pinot Noir; Savagnin, Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc; Poulsard, Trousseau, Pinot Noir
- **Aging:** 2–8 years; 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Daniel Dugois, Raphaël Fumey & Adeline

#### ARBOIS MOUSSEUX AOC

This is a traditional method sparkling wine seldom seen outside Arbois.

- **Sparkling style:** WHITE These fresh, dry sparkling wines show more potential than quality.
- **Grapes:** Savagnin, Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Foret, Fruitière Vinicole d’Arbois

#### ARBOIS PUPILLIN AOC

This is a single-commune appellation for red, white, rosé, vin jaune, and vin de paille made to the same specification as Arbois AOC.

- **Producers:** Désiré Petit & Fils (Vin de Paille), De la

#### ARBOIS VIN DE PAILLE AOC

This wine is made from grapes that are dried to concentrate the juice, followed by a long fermentation and up to four years in wood.

- **White style:** These are very sweet wines with an old-gold color, complex bouquet, and a rich, nutty flavor.
- **Grapes:** Poulsard, Trousseau, Savagnin, Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 10–50 (or more) years
- **Producers:** Rolet, André & Mireille Tissot Y

#### ARBOIS VIN JAUNE AOC

For details of production and style, see Château-Chalon AOC. André & Mireille Tissot Y• Jacques Tissot

- **Producers:** De la Pinte Y, Jacques Puffeney, Rolet

#### AUXERROIS AOC

This is not an AOC in itself, but a subordinate designation that may be appended either to the basic appellation or to Alsace Grand Cru AOC.


#### AUXEY-DURESSES AOC

A beautiful village, set in an idyllic valley behind Monthélie and Meursault.

- **Red style:** Attractive wines not very deep in color, but with a softness of fruit and a little finesse.
- **White style:** Medium-bodied wines with a full, spicynutty flavor, like that of a modest Meursault. Henri Latour • Olivier Leflaive • Leroy B • Prunier (all and sundry) • Dominique & Vincent Roy • Jean-Marc Vincent (Les Hautes) AUXEY-DURESSES
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 6–12 years; 3–7 years
- **Producers:** Robert Ampeau, d’Auvenay B, JeanPierre Diconne, Alain Gras, Louis Jadot

#### AUXEY-DURESSESCÔTES DE BEAUNE AOC

Alternative appellation for red wines only. See Auxey-Duresses AOC.


#### AZAY-LE-RIDEAU AOC

Good-quality wines from eight villages on each side of the Indre River, a tributary of the Loire.

- **White style:** Delicate, light-bodied wines that are usually dry but may be demi-sec. ROSÉ Attractive, refreshing, truly dry (maximum of 3 grams per liter residual sugar) wines that are coral-pink and often have a strawberry aroma. Gamay, Cabernet Franc, and Cabernet Sauvignon (the last two…
- **Grapes:** Chenin Blanc; A minimum of 60% Grolleau, plus Malbec,
- **Aging:** 1–2 years
- **Producers:** J.L. Page, Pibaleau Père & Fils

#### BARSAC AOC

The commune of Barsac is one of five that have the right to the Sauternes appellation. (The others are Preignac, Fargues, Bommes, and Sauternes itself.) Some generic wines sold in bulk may take advantage of this, but all individual properties are sold as Barsac.

- **White style:** Luscious, intensely sweet wines similar in style to Sauternes, but perhaps lighter in weight, slightly drier, and less rich. As in Sauternes, 1983 is one of the best vintages of the 20th century. years for the greatest
- **Grapes:** Sémillon, Sauvignon Blanc, Muscadelle
- **Aging:** 6–25 years for most wines; between 15–60

#### BÂTARD-MONTRACHET AOC

Grand Cru This grand cru is situated on the slope beneath Le Montrachet and overlaps both ChassagneMontrachet and Puligny-Montrachet.

- **White style:** Full-bodied, intensely rich wine with masses of nutty, honey-and-toast flavors. It is one of the best dry white wines in the world. Jean-Noël Gagnard • Vincent Girardin • Louis Latour • Leflaive B • Marc Morey • Pierre Morey B • Michel Niellon • Paul Pernot • Ramonet • Antonin…
- **Grapes:** Pinot chardonnay (sic)
- **Aging:** 8–20 years
- **Producers:** Jean-Marc Boillot, Bouchard Père & Fils

#### BÉARN AOC

This modest AOC shines in the local Basque area. Wines made in Bellocq, Lahontan, Orthez, and Saliès are allowed to add the communal designation Bellocq to the appellation.

- **Red style:** Fresh, light, and fruity wines with a good balance, but lacking depth. Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon, Fer, Manseng Noir, Courbu Noir
- **White style:** Light, dry, and aromatic wines. Blanc, Lauzet, Camaralet, Raffiat, Sauvignon Blanc ROSÉ Simple, fruity, dry rosés with a fresh floral aroma. Sauvignon, Fer, Manseng Noir, Courbu Noir (Larribère)
- **Grapes:** A maximum of 60% Tannat, plus Cabernet; Petit Manseng, Gros Manseng, Courbu; Tannat, Cabernet Franc, Cabernet
- **Aging:** 1–4 years; 1–2 years
- **Producers:** Bouscassé (Rosé), C. V. de Jurançon

#### BÉARN-BELLOCQ AOC

See Béarn AOC


#### BEAUJOLAIS AOC

This generic Beaujolais appellation accounts for half the wine produced in the district and more than half of this is sold as Beaujolais “Primeur.” The basic quality of these wines means that they cannot be bought from the great négociants of the Côte d’Or, although cru Beaujolai

- **Red style:** Due to their method of vinification, most of these wines have a “pear-drop” character to their fruitiness. The best also have a delightful freshness and frankness that beg for the wine to be consumed in large quantities.
- **White style:** Less than half of one percent of the production of this basic appellation consists of dry white wine, and the specialized producers of this usually make very fine wines. Pierre Charmet’s Beaujolais blanc is aromatic and peachy. ROSÉ Fresh, “pretty,” and fruity. (Les Bruyères) •…
- **Grapes:** Gamay, Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris; Chardonnay, Aligoté
- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Jean-Paul Brun (Terres Dorées), JeanJacques Martin (white), Cave Sain-Bel

#### BEAUMES-DE-VENISE AOC

These wines from Muscat de Beaumes-deVenise are the most elegant of the world’s sweet fortified Muscat wines. Very little sweet Muscat was made before World War II.

- **White style:** /ROSÉ The color varies between the rare pale gold and the common light apricotgold, with an aromatic bouquet more akin to the perfume of dried flowers than fruit. You should expect Muscat de Beaumes-de-Venise to have hardly any acidity, which surprisingly does not make it…
- **Grapes:** Muscat Blanc à Petits Grains, Muscat Rosé à
- **Aging:** 1–2 years

#### BEAUNE AOC

Beaune gives its name to village wines and premiers crus, but not to any grands crus.

- **Red style:** These soft-scented, gently fruity wines are consistent and good value.
- **White style:** An uncomplicated dry Chardonnay wine with a characteristic soft finish. Dominique Laurent (Vieilles Vignes) • Machard de Gramont • Tollot-Beaut • de la Vougeraie B
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 6–14 years; 3–7 years
- **Producers:** Bouchard Père & Fils, Bertrand Darviot

#### BEAUNE PREMIER CRU AOC

Premiers crus: Les Aigrots, Aux Coucherias (or Clos de la Féguine), Aux Cras, Clos des Avaux, Les Avaux, Le Bas des Teurons, Les Beaux Fougets, Belissand, Les Blanches Fleurs, Les Boucherottes, Les Bressandes, Les Cents Vignes, Champs Pimont, Les Chouacheux, l’Écu (or Clos de l’É

- **Red style:** The best crus are medium-bodied with a delightfully soft rendition of Pinot fruit and lots of finesse. Look out for Faiveley’s Clos de L’Ecu, a monopole purchased at the time of writing. I expect Faiveley will want to make some sort of quality statement here.
- **White style:** These wines have lovely finesse and can display a toasty flavor more common to richer growths. Domaine Jacques Prieur’s Champs Pimont has stood out lately. Chanson Père & Fils (Clos de Fèves, Clos des Mouches) • Joseph Drouhin • Camille Giroud • Louis Jadot • Jaffelin • Michel…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 10–20 years; 5–12 years
- **Producers:** Arnoux Père & Fils, Bouchard Père & Fils

#### BERGERAC AOC

Adjoining Bordeaux, Bergerac produces wines that are sometimes mistaken for the modest appellations of its more famous neighbor.

- **Red style:** The best reds have a good garnet or ruby color, fine fruit, and an elegant balance. Malbec, Fer, Mérille
- **White style:** Mostly dry Bordeaux-style wines, but semisweet wines with up to 54 grams per liter of residual sugar are permitted. Until 1993, such wines had to be described as moelleux; however, as this is no longer compulsory, we will not know whether some of these wines are moelleux or not.…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc, Merlot,; Sémillon, Sauvignon Blanc, Muscadelle,
- **Aging:** 2–8 years; 1–3 years

#### BERGERAC SEC AOC

These white wines are distinguished from Bergerac Blanc by having to be dry, with no more than 4 grams per liter of residual sugar.

- **White style:** Dry Bordeaux-style wines. They may be sold from December 1 following the harvest without any mention of primeur or nouveau. Ondenc, Chenin Blanc, and up to 25% Ugni Blanc (on the proviso that the quantity of Sauvignon Blanc used is at least equal) tde l’Ancienne Cure • Château…
- **Grapes:** Sémillon, Sauvignon Blanc, Muscadelle,
- **Aging:** 1–3 years

#### BIENVENUES-BÂTARD-MONTRACHET AOC

Grand Cru This is one of Puligny-Montrachet’s four grands crus.

- **White style:** Not the fattest dry wines from this village, but they have great finesse, immaculate balance, and some of the nuttiness and honeyand-toast flavors expected in all Montrachets. Trebuchet • Vincent Girardin • Louis Latour • Leflaive B • Paul Pernot • Ramonet • Étienne Sauzet
- **Grapes:** Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 8–20 years
- **Producers:** Louis Carillon & Fils, Chartron et

#### BLAGNY AOC

A red-only appellation from Blagny, a tiny hamlet shared by the communes of Meursault and Puligny-Montrachet.

- **Red style:** These rich, full-flavored, Meursault-like red wines are underrated.
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault
- **Aging:** 8–15 years
- **Producers:** Robert Ampeau, Matrot

#### BLAGNY PREMIER CRU AOC

Premiers crus: La Garenne (or Sur la Garenne), Hameau de Blagny (in PulignyMontrachet), La Jeunelotte, La Pièce sous le Bois, Sous Blagny (in Meursault), Sous le Dos d’Ane, Sous le Puits.

- **Red style:** These rich wines have even more grip and attack than basic Blagny. (Sous le Puits) • Leflaive B • Matrot BLAGNY-CÔTE
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault
- **Aging:** 10–20 years
- **Producers:** Robert Ampeau, François Jobard, Larue

#### BLAYAIS AOC

See Blaye AOC


#### BLAYE AOC

Blaye or Blayais is a large and diverse appellation of variable quality.

- **Red style:** Few properties cultivate obscure varieties such as Prolongeau and Béguignol, thus many utilize the prestigious sounding Premières Côtes de Blaye AOC; hence, hardly anyone bothers to sell the wine under this plain appellation. Malbec, Prolongeau, Béguignol, Petit Verdot
- **White style:** Since 1997 Ugni Blanc has dominated, with Merlot Blanc and Folle Blanche banned. The ripeness level has been lowered from 170 to 153 grams of sugar, with no more than four grams residual allowed in the finished wine, and a maximum of 12.5 percent alcohol imposed, thus ensuring…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc, Merlot,; Ugni Blanc plus up to 10% in total of Folle
- **Aging:** 3–7 years; 1–2 years

#### BONNES MARES AOC

The other Bourgogne lieu-dit in this district is actually located on the outskirts of Dijon. Some might think this is a wine to moon over, but the only wine I have tasted (again Domaine Bouvier) was again not special.

- **Red style:** This wine combines a fabulous femininity of style with sheer depth of flavor to give something rich and luscious, yet complex and complete. • Dominique Laurent • J. F. Mugnier • Christophe Roumier • Georges Roumier • Nicolas Potel • Comte Georges de Vogüé BOURGOGNE MONTRECUL OR…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault
- **Aging:** 12–25 years
- **Producers:** Bouchard Père & Fils, Dujac, R. Groffier

#### BONNEZEAUX AOC

Grown on three south-facing river slopes of the commune of Thouarcé in the Coteaux du Layon, this is one of the undisputed great sweet T H E L O I R E VA L L E Y wines of France.

- **White style:** Intensely sweet, richer, and more fullbodied than Quarts-de-Chaume, the other great growth of the Layon Valley, this wine can have pineapple and liquorice fruit when young, often achieving a beautiful honeyed-vanilla complexity with age. Godineau/Des Petit Quarts • Claude Robin…
- **Grapes:** Chenin Blanc
- **Aging:** Up to 20 years or more
- **Producers:** Philippe Delesvaux, Château de Fesles

#### BORDEAUX-CÔTES-DEFRANCS LIQUOREUX AOC

This style of Bordeaux-Côtes-de-Francs wine must by law be naturally sweet and made from overripe grapes that possess at least 223 grams of sugar per liter.

- **White style:** Rich, genuinely liquoreux wines; only tiny amounts are made.
- **Grapes:** Sauvignon Blanc, Sémillon, Muscadelle
- **Aging:** 5–15 years

#### BORDEAUXCÔTES-DE-FRANCS AOC

This forgotten area’s vineyards are contiguous with those of Puisseguin-St.-Émilion and LussacSt.-Émilion, and have a very similar claylimestone over limestone and iron-pan soil.

- **Red style:** These are essentially robust, rustic, fullbodied wines that are softened by their high Merlot content. Malbec, Merlot
- **White style:** Little-seen dry, semisweet, and sweet wines of clean, fruity character.
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon,; Sauvignon Blanc, Sémillon, Muscadelle
- **Aging:** 5–10 years

#### BOURG AOC

Also called Bourgeais, this appellation, which covers both red and white wines, has fallen into disuse because the growers prefer to use the Côtes de Bourg AOC, which is easier to market but conforms to the same regulations.


#### BOURGEAIS AOC

See Bourg AOC


#### BOURGOGNE HAUTESCÔTES DE BEAUNE AOC

This appellation is larger and more varied than Hautes-Côtes de Nuits. Half-red/half-rosé wines may be sold as Bourgogne Clairet Hautes-Côtes de Beaune AOC, but the style is outmoded and the appellation rarely encountered.

- **Red style:** Ruby-colored, medium-bodied wines with a Pinot perfume and a creamy-fruit finish.
- **White style:** Not very frequently encountered, but Guillemard-Dupont’s pure Pinot Beurot (Pinot Gris) is rich, dry, and expressive. Its grape variety makes it a very pale white, red, or rosé according to the regulations! ROSÉ Pleasantly dry and fruity wines with some richness and a soft…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 4–10 years; 1–4 years; 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Contat-Grange, Jean Joliot & Fils, Didier

#### BOURGOGNE HAUTESCÔTES DE NUITS AOC

A source of good-value wines, these vineyards have expanded since the 1970s, and the quality is improving noticeably. Half-red/half-rosé wines may be sold as Bourgogne Clairet Hautes-Côtes de Nuits AOC, but the style is outmoded and the appellation rarely encountered.

- **Red style:** Medium-bodied and medium- to fullbodied wines with good fruit and some true Côte de Nuits character. The wines from some growers have fine oak nuances.
- **White style:** Just 5 percent of the production is dry white. Most have a good weight of fruit, but little finesse. ROSÉ Little-seen, but those that have cropped up have been dry, fruity, and delicious wines of some richness.
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Liébault, Pinot Gris; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 4–10 years; 1–4 years; 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Bertagna, J.-C. Boisset, Yves Chaley, Guy

#### BOURGUEIL AOC

Most of the vines are grown on a sand-andgravel plateau, or terrasse, by the river. The wines have a pronounced fruity character and are delicious to drink when less than six months old.

- **Red style:** Bone-dry to dry, medium-bodied, lively wines, full of soft-fruit flavors, which are often aged in cask. They are very easy to drink when less than six months old; many close up when in bottle and need time to soften. Wines from the terrasse vineyards are best drunk young, while…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc with up to 10% Cabernet
- **Aging:** Within 6 months or after 6 years; 2–3 years
- **Producers:** Yannick Amirault (Les Quartier Vieilles

#### BOUZERON AOC

In 1979 Bouzeron became the only cru to have its own appellation specifically for the Aligoté grape, as Bourgogne Aligoté Bouzeron AOC, and in 1998 achieved full village status.

- **White style:** This excellent and interesting dry wine is much the fullest version of Aligoté available. In weight, fruit, and spice its style is nearer to Pinot Gris than to Chardonnay. The Bouzeron produced by A. & P. de Villaine is a class apart. Père & Fils) • Chanzy Frères • André Delorme…
- **Grapes:** Aligoté
- **Aging:** 1–5 years
- **Producers:** Bougeot, Ancien Carnot (sold by Bouchard

#### BRISSAC AOC

This village was singled out under the Anjou-Villages appellation in 1998 and backdated for wines from 1996 onward. It covers the area of Brissac-Quincé and nine surrounding communes.

- **Producers:** Davieu (Château de Brissac), De HautePerche, Du Prieuré, Richou, Des

#### BUGEY VDQS

Formerly called Vin de Bugey, the criteria for these wines has changed substantially since the decree of January 2004, particularly the encépagement.

- **Red style:** Fresh wines that range from the fruity Pinot to the rich Mondeuse.
- **White style:** Off-dry, fresh, light, and gently fruity. optional Aligoté, Altesse, Jacquère, Mondeuse Blanche, and Pinot Gris ROSÉ Light and refreshing dry wines.
- **Grapes:** Gamay, Mondeuse, Pinot Noir; A minimum of 50% Chardonnay, plus
- **Aging:** 2–8 years; 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Monin (Mondeuse: Les Griots, Les Perrailles)

#### BUGEY CERDON VDQS

This appellation was a confusing mess until January 2004. Prior to this date, the wines could be still or sparkling, dry or sweet, and white or red.

- **Sparkling style:** ROSÉ Should be fresh and luscious, but true Cerdon Rosé Méthode Ancestrale has yet to establish a track record.
- **Grapes:** Gamay, Poulsard
- **Aging:** Within 1 year
- **Producers:** Daniel Boccard, Christian Bolliet

#### BUZET AOC

Formerly known as Côtes de Buzet, this supervalue Bordeaux satellite is located on the northern edge of the Armagnac region.

- **Red style:** The best are always very good, with considerable finesse and charm. Malbec
- **White style:** These dry whites are the least interesting wines in this appellation. They may be sold from December 1 following the harvest without any mention of primeur or nouveau. ROSÉ Ripe, fruity, dry rosés. They may be sold from December 1 following the harvest without any mention of…
- **Grapes:** Merlot, Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,; Sémillon, Sauvignon Blanc, Muscadelle

#### CABERNET D’ANJOU AOC

This appellation includes Saumur, and it was a saumurois named Taveau who, in 1905, was the first person to make an Anjou Rosé from Cabernet grapes.

- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon
- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** La Croix des Loges, C. V. de la Loire (Elysis)

#### CABERNET DE SAUMUR AOC

All Cabernet de Saumur wines have the right to claim the appellation Cabernet d’Anjou, but those sold as Saumur are usually finer in quality.

- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon
- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Du Val Brun

#### CAHORS AOC

The once-famous “black wine” of Cahors got its name from the Malbec grape, which, prior to phylloxera, produced dark, inky-colored wines.

- **Red style:** Should be richer than Bergerac AOC. Malbec, Fer, Mérille Fûts de Chêne) • Château Les Mailleries SOUTHWEST (Dany Moelleux) • Château Masburel • Marlene & Alain Mayet (Révelation du Bois de Pourquie) • Château Tour des Verdots

#### CAIRANNE AOC

Orsan, and St.-Etienne-des-Sorts. These villages are situated just north of Lirac and Tavel, two famous rosé appellations, and make an excellent rosé.

- **Producers:** C. V. de Chusclan (Esprit de Terror), Clavel (L’Étoile du Berger), C. V. de St.Gervais (Prestige), Sainte-Anne

#### CANON-FRONSAC AOC

See Côtes-Canon-Fronsac AOC


#### CARAMANY AOC

This supervalue red wine conforms to the requirements of Côtes du Roussillon Villages, except for a minimum Syrah content and the stipulation that the Carignan must be vinified by macération carbonique.

- **Red style:** Simply the fullest, richest, and longestliving wines of Roussillon, despite the macération carbonique, which just goes to prove how useful that process can be when used to lift the natural fruit of a wine instead of dominating it with pear-drop aromas. that there must be at…
- **Grapes:** As for Côtes du Roussillon Villages except
- **Aging:** 3–15 years

#### CASSIS AOC

This is a decent but overpriced Provence appellation located around a beautiful rocky bay, a few miles east of Marseille.

- **Red style:** These solid, well-colored red wines can age, but most do not improve. Barbaroux, plus up to 10% (in total) of Terret Noir, Terret Gris, Terret Blanc, Terret Ramenée, Aramon, and Aramon Gris
- **White style:** These dry white wines have an interesting bouquet of herby aromas of gorse and bracken, but are usually flabby and unbalanced on the palate. However, Bagnol and St.-Magdeleine produce excellent, racy whites for such southerly vineyards, and Ferme Blanche can be almost as good.…
- **Grapes:** Grenache, Carignan, Mourvèdre, Cinsault,; Ugni Blanc, Sauvignon Blanc, Doucillon,
- **Aging:** All wines: 1–3 years

#### CÉRONS AOC

These inexpensive wines from an area adjacent to Barsac are the best value-for-money sweet wines in Bordeaux. They must include overripe botrytized grapes harvested in tries.

- **Red style:** Soft, silky reds of great violety elegance, and not lacking either concentration or length. Most have been aged in a percentage of new oak, which adds a smoky or tobaccolike complexity. Malbec, Petit Verdot, Carmenère
- **White style:** The serious styles are invariably oaked these days, with oodles of flavor, often tropical and fruity, with a firm acid structure. These wines may be sold from December 1 following the harvest without any mention of primeur or nouveau. Sémillon, Muscadelle the best
- **Grapes:** Sémillon, Sauvignon Blanc, Muscadelle; Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc, Merlot; Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc, Merlot,; A minimum of 25% Sauvignon Blanc, plus
- **Aging:** 6–15 years for most wines; 6–15 years; 1–2 years for modest wines; 8–20 years for; 6–20 years; Usually 3–8 years, but up to 20 years for

#### CHALONNAISE AOC

As from the 1990 vintage, basic Bourgogne made exclusively from grapes harvested in the region may bear this specific appellation.

- **Red style:** A. & P. de Villaine’s Digoine has outstanding fruit and finesse for such a modest appellation. Only Michel Goubard’s Mont-Avril comes close in richness, and can be truly excellent, but is not in the same class. Both are terrific value.
- **White style:** Normally softer and fuller than a Mâcon, but not particularly rich in fruit, except for A. & P. de Villaine’s Clous, which is the best white wine in the appellation, and Venot’s La Corvée, the number two. ROSÉ Seldom encountered and not special; no reason though why it should…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Liébault, Pinot Gris; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 1–3 years; Upon purchase
- **Producers:** René Bourgeon, Caves de Buxy, André

#### CHARLEMAGNE AOC

Grand Cru This white-only grand cru of Aloxe-Corton overlaps Pernand-Vergelesses and is almost, but not quite, identical to the grand cru of CortonCharlemagne.


#### CHARMES-CHAMBERTIN AOC

Another Gevrey-Chambertin grand cru. The wine may be sold simply as Chambertin, the name of a neighboring grand cru, but Chambertin may not call itself Clos de Bèze.

#### CHÂTEAU-CHALON AOC

The Jura’s vins jaunes possess a unique style and Château-Chalon (which is the name of a commune) is its most legendary exponent.

- **White style:** Vin jaune is something of an acquired taste and should be drunk when very old. The vast array of complex nuances of bouquet eventually subdue and transmute the sherrylike smell of acetaldehyde. It has a dry flavor. Philippe Butin • Durand-Perron • Jean Macle
- **Grapes:** Savagnin
- **Aging:** 10–100 years
- **Producers:** Baud Père & Fils, Berthet-Bondet

#### CHÂTEAUNEUF-DU-PAPE AOC

The name Châteauneuf-du-Pape dates from the time of the dual papacy in the 14th century.

- **Red style:** Due to the variations of terroir and almost limitless permutations of encépagement, it is impossible to describe a typical Châteauneufdu-Pape, but there are two categories—the traditional, full, dark, spicy, long-lived style and the modern, easy-drinking Châteauneufdu-Pape, the…
- **White style:** Early harvesting has reduced sugar levels and increased acidity, while modern vinification techniques have encouraged a drop in fermentation temperatures, so these wines are not as full as those produced previously. They can still be very rich, albeit in a more opulent, exotic…
- **Grapes:** Grenache, Syrah, Mourvèdre, Picpoul, Terret
- **Aging:** nown as le rapé.; 6–25 years; 1–3 years (4–6 years in exceptional cases)
- **Producers:** Paul Autard (Côte Ronde), Barville

#### CHÂTILLON-EN-DIOIS AOC

This wine was raised to full AOC status in 1974, although it is hard to comprehend what merited such an elevation then or now.

- **Red style:** A garnet-colored wine of full body, fire, and power, made fragrant by the addition of Viognier grapes. The result is a long-living and complex wine with nuances of violets and spices, and great finesse. YtPatrick & Christophe Bonnefond • CluselRoch Y • Cuilleron-Gaillard-Villard…
- **White style:** These pale-gold-colored wines are essentially dry, but can have such an exotic perfume that you might easily think they are sweet when you first breathe in their heavenly aroma. The bouquet is fine, floral, and fruity, with heady, opulent aromas that can include may-blossom,…
- **Grapes:** Gamay, plus up to 25% Syrah and; Aligoté, Chardonnay; Gamay, plus up to 25% Syrah and Pinot; Viognier; Syrah, plus up to 20% Viognier
- **Aging:** 4–8 years; 10–25 years
- **Producers:** Didier Cornillon (Clos de Beylière), Yves Cuilleron (Les Ayguets), Delas (Clos

#### CHEVERNY AOC

Upgraded from VDQS to full AOC status in 1993.

- **Red style:** Dry, light- to medium-bodied wines. The smaller growers mostly produce pure Gamay wines of very acceptable quality, although the addition of 10 percent or more Pinot Noir gives a smoother wine. Gamay Teinturier de Chaudenay, which has colored juice, is no longer permitted. Noir,…
- **White style:** Dry, light-bodied, modest wines with a fine, flowery nose, delicate flavor, and crisp balance. Now primarily a Sauvignon Blanc wine, as Romorantin may not be used and the amount of Sauvignon in these wines has been regulated since 1993. Chardonnay, Chenin Blanc, and Arbois ROSÉ…
- **Grapes:** Between 40 and 65% Gamay, plus Pinot; Between 65 and 80% Sauvignon Blanc, plus; At least 50% Gamay, plus Cabernet Franc,
- **Aging:** nown. Cheverny is usually made and marketed; 1–2 years
- **Producers:** De la Desoucherie, De la Gaudronnière

#### CHINON AOC

The appellations of Chinon and Bourgueil produce the best red wine in Touraine using the Cabernet Franc grape, known locally as Breton.

- **Red style:** Bone-dry to dry, light- to medium-bodied wines that are lively, soft, and delicate. Most growers use small oak casks for aging and produce wines of very good quality. Sauvignon
- **White style:** A tiny production of clean, dry, lightto medium-bodied wines that are strangely aromatic for Chenin Blanc with an intriguing perfumed aftertaste. ROSÉ These are dry, fairly light-bodied, smooth, and fruity wines which, like Bourgueil rosés, are very easy to drink and deserve to…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc with up to 10% Cabernet; Chenin Blanc
- **Aging:** 2–3 years; 1–2 years
- **Producers:** Philippe Alliet, Château de l’Aulée

#### CHOREY-LÈS-BEAUNE AOC

This satellite appellation of Beaune produces exciting, underrated wines.

- **Red style:** Although next to Aloxe-Corton, Chorey has all the soft and sensuous charms that are quintessentially Beaune.
- **White style:** Less than one percent of the wines produced in this village are white. Maurice Martray • Tollot-Beaut CHOREY-LÈS-BEAUNE
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault
- **Aging:** 7–15 years
- **Producers:** Arnoux Père & Fils, Maillard Père & Fils

#### CHUSCLAN AOC

One of two single-village appellations whose wines actually come from more than one commune, Chusclan may also be made from vines growing in Bagnols-sur-Cèze, Cadolet, CÔTES DU RHÔNE

NUITS required for a completely harmonious flavor.

#### CLOS DE TART AOC

Grand Cru This is one of the four grands crus of Morey-St.Denis. It is entirely owned by the négociant Mommessin. In addition to Clos de Tart itself, a tiny part of the Bonnes Mares grand cru also has the right to this appellation.

- **Red style:** This monopole yields wines with a penetrating Pinot flavor, to which Mommessin adds such a spicy-vanilla character from 100 percent new oak that great bottle maturity is

#### CLOS DE VOUGEOT AOC

An alternative appellation for Chambertin-Clos de Bèze. See Chambertin-Clos de Bèze AOC.


#### CLOS DES LAMBRAYS AOC

Grand Cru Covering an area of almost 42 acres (17 hectares), Clos de La Roche is twice the size of the other grands crus of Morey-St.-Denis.

- **Red style:** A deep-colored, rich, and powerfully flavored vin de garde with a silky texture. Many consider it the greatest grand cru of Morey-St.-Denis. Hubert Lignier • Michel Magnien & Fils • Ponsot • Armand Rousseau
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault
- **Aging:** 10–20 years
- **Producers:** Dujac, Leroy B, Lucien Lemoine

#### CLOS ST.-DENIS AOC

Grand Cru This is the grand cru that the village of Morey attached to its name when it was the best growth in the village, a position now contested by Clos de la Roche and Clos de Tart.

- **Red style:** Strong, fine, and firm wines with rich liquorice and berry flavors that require time to come together. Philippe Charlopin • Dujac • Louis Jadot • Lucien Lemoine • Michel Magnien & Fils • Ponsot • Nicolas Potel
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault
- **Aging:** 10–25 years
- **Producers:** Bertagna, Philippe Charlopin-Parizot

#### CLOS VOUGEOT AOC

See Clos de Vougeot AOC


#### CONDRIEU AOC


#### CORNAS AOC

The sun-trap vineyards of Cornas produce the best value of all the Rhône’s quality red wines, but you have to buy it as soon as it is released as it is always sold far too young, so there is none left when it starts to show its true potential.

- **Red style:** Ink-black, full-bodied, strong-flavored, pure Syrah wines with lots of blackcurrant and blackberry fruit, lacking only a little finesse if compared to a great Hermitage or Côte Rôtie. Joël Durand • Patrick Lesec • Paul Jaboulet Aîné • Tardieu-Laurent (Vieilles Vignes)
- **Grapes:** Syrah
- **Aging:** 7–20 years
- **Producers:** Thierry Allemand, Auguste Clape, JeanLuc Colombo, Courbis (Les Eygats), Eric &

#### CORSE AOC

Alternative appellation, see Vin de Corse AOC


#### CORTON AOC

Grand Cru This is one of the grands crus of Aloxe-Corton (it extends into Ladoix-Serrigny and PernandVergelesses). Corton is the only grand cru in the Côte de Beaune that includes red and white wines and thus parallels the Côte de Nuits grand cru of Musigny.

- **Red style:** These wines may sometimes appear intense and broody in their youth, but, when fully mature, a great Corton has such finesse and complexity that it can stun the senses.
- **White style:** A medium- to full-bodied wine with a fine, rich flavor. Chevalier Père & Fils (Rognet) • Vincent Girardin • Antonin Guyon • Louis Jadot • Leroy B • Bonneau du Martray • Faiveley • Michel Juillot • Prince Florent de Mérode • Jacques Prieur • Rapet Père & Fils • Comte Daniel…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 12–30 years; 10–25 years
- **Producers:** Arnoux Père & Fils, Bouchard Père & Fils

#### CORTON-CHARLEMAGNE AOC

Grand Cru BURGUNDY - THE CÔTE DE BEAUNE AND HAUTES-CÔTES This famous grand cru of Aloxe-Corton extends into Ladoix-Serrigny and Pernand-Vergelesses.

- **White style:** At its natural best, this is the most sumptuous of all white Burgundies. It has a fabulous concentration of rich, buttery fruit flavors, a dazzling balance of acidity, and delicious overtones of vanilla, honey, and cinnamon. • Bouchard Père & Fils • J.-F. Coche-Dury • Joseph…
- **Grapes:** Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 5–25 years
- **Producers:** Bertrand Ambroise, Bonneau du Martray

#### COSNE-SUR-LOIRE AOC

Of the 16 communes that produce Coteaux du Giennois, only eight are entitled to add Cosnesur-Loire to the appellation. It was promoted from VDQS to AOC in 1998.

- **Red style:** Dry, bright ruby-colored wines. Often fulltasting, but rarely with the body to match and sometimes quite tannic. variety may exceed 80% of the total blend
- **White style:** These are dry, medium-bodied, curiously aromatic white wines that develop a full, toasty flavor with a little bottle age. ROSÉ Light-salmon-colored wines, slightly fuller bodied than the Coteaux du Giennois made a little further to the north, but equally capable of the same…
- **Grapes:** Gamay, Pinot Noir—since 1992, neither; Sauvignon Blanc
- **Aging:** 1–2 years; 1–4 years
- **Producers:** Alain Paulat Y

#### CÔTE DE BEAUNE AOC

Wines that are entitled to the actual Côte de Beaune appellation are restricted to a few plots on the Montagne de Beaune above Beaune itself.

- **Red style:** These are fine, stylish wines that reveal the purest of Pinot Noir fruit. They are produced in the soft Beaune style.
- **White style:** Little-seen, dry basic Beaune.
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 10–20 years; 3–8 years
- **Producers:** Lycée Agricole & Viticole de Beaune

#### CÔTE DE BEAUNEVILLAGES AOC

While AOC Côte de Nuits-Villages covers red and white wines in a predominantly red-wine district, AOC Côte de Beaune-Villages applies only to red wines in a district that produces the greatest white Burgundies!

- **Red style:** Excellent-value fruity wines, made in true soft Beaune style. • Lequin Roussot
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault
- **Aging:** 7–15 years
- **Producers:** Bernard Bachelet & Fils, Coron Père & Fils

#### CÔTE DE NUITS-VILLAGES AOC

This appellation covers the wines produced in one or more of five communes: Fixin and Brochon situated in the north of the district and Comblanchien, Corgoloin, and Prissy in the south.

- **Red style:** Firm, fruity, and distinctive wines made in true, well-structured Côte de Nuits style.
- **White style:** Very little is made—just 44 cases (4 hectoliters) in 1985—and I have never encountered it. tde l'Arlot • Michel Esmonin • Gachot-Monot • Naudin-Ferrand • Daniel Rion & Fils (Le Vaucrain)
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 6–10 years

Nonvintage and vintage The still wines (red, white, and rosé) that are produced in Champagne are low in quality and high in price.

#### COTEAUX DE DIE AOC

This new appellation was created in 1993 to soak up wines from excess Clairette grape production that were formerly sold under previous sparkling-wine appellations.

- **White style:** Perhaps it is unfair to prejudge a wine that has had so little time to establish itself, but it is hard to imagine a quality wine made from this grape. In effect, Coteaux de Die is the stillwine version of Crémant de Die. It is easy to see why such a bland dry wine needs bubbles…
- **Grapes:** Clairette
- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Jean-Claude Raspail

#### COTEAUX DE L’AUBANCE AOC

These wines are made from old vines grown on the schistous banks of the Aubance River.

#### COTEAUX DU GIENNOIS AOC

This appellation could boast nearly a thousand growers at the turn of the 20th century when it was 40 times its current size.

- **Red style:** Dry, light-bodied red wines that often have less color than many rosés. variety may exceed 80% of the total blend
- **White style:** These very basic, dry, light-bodied wines lack interest. Chenin Blanc can no longer be used to make this wine. ROSÉ Light-salmon-colored, light-bodied wines that can have a fragrant citrous character. variety may exceed 80% of the total blend

#### COTEAUX DU LAYON AOC

This appellation, which overlaps Anjou Coteaux de la Loire in the northwest and Saumur in the southeast, has been famous for its sweet white wines since the fourth century.

#### CÔTES AOC

These are dry white wines that come from 11 communes. The grapes must be riper than for ordinary Gaillac AOC and the wine must conform to the technical requirements of Gaillac Doux in all but sweetness.

- **Producers:** Robert Plageoles, Château de Salettes

#### CÔTES DE BLAYE AOC

Unlike the Bourg and Côtes de Bourg appellations, which cover red and white wines, Côtes de Blaye is white only. Blaye, however, may be red or white.

#### CÔTES DE BOURG AOC

Bourg is one-fifth the size of Blaye, yet it traditionally produces a greater quantity and, more importantly, a much finer quality of wine than that produced at Blaye.

- **Red style:** Excellent value wines of good color, which are full of solid, fruity flavor. Many are very stylish indeed. Merlot, Malbec
- **White style:** A very small quantity of this light, dry wine is produced and sold each year. It may be sold from December 1 following the harvest without any mention of primeur or nouveau. Merlot Blanc, Colombard, plus a maximum of 10% Chenin Blanc PREMIÈRES CÔTES

- **Aging:** 3–10 years; 1–2 years

An appellation of increasing interest.

#### FITOU AOC

When it was made an AOC in 1948, Fitou was fast asleep, but wine buyers beat a path to its desolate door in the early 1980s, making it the fastest-rising star in the Mediterranean firmament.

- **Red style:** Even at the lowest level, these wines have a fine color and a spicy warmth of Grenache that curbs and softens the concentrated fruit and tannin of low-yielding Carignan. Mourvèdre, Syrah, and at least 30% Grenache and Lladoner Pelut; within this Carignan, Grenache, and Lladoner…

- **Aging:** 3–6 years (4–10 years in exceptional cases)

#### FIXIN AOC

Fixin was at one time the summer residence of the dukes of Burgundy.

- **Red style:** Well-colored wines that can be firm, tannic vins de garde of excellent quality and even better value.
- **White style:** Rich, dry, and concentrated wines that are rare, but exciting and well worth seeking out. Bruno Clair shows what Pinot Blanc can produce when it is not overcropped. Gelin • Philippe Joliet • Mongeard-Mugneret
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc

- **Red style:** Splendidly deep in color and full in body with masses of blackcurrant and redcurrant fruit, supported by a good tannic structure.
- **White style:** I have not encountered any but it would not be unreasonable to assume that it might be at least as good as a basic Fixin blanc. Mongeard-Mugneret
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault

#### GIVRY AOC

Underrated wines from just south of Mercurey.

- **Red style:** Light- to medium-bodied, soft, and fruity wine with delightful nuances of cherry and redcurrant.
- **White style:** Just 10 percent of Givry is white—a deliciously clean, dry Chardonnay that can have an attractive spicy-buttery hint on the aftertaste. Michel Derain • Didier Erker (En Chenèvre) • Mme du Jardin • Joblot • Louis Latour • François Lumpp • Parize Père & Fils (Champ Nalot) •…

- **Red style:** Best examples are medium bodied, soft, rich, and fruity with delightful nuances of cherry and redcurrant.

#### JASNIÈRES AOC

This is the best area of the Coteaux du Loir— the wines produced here can, in hot years, achieve a richness that compares well with those of Savennières in Anjou (see p.206).

#### LATRICIÈRESCHAMBERTIN AOC

- **Red style:** Solid structure and a certain austerity connect the two different styles of this wine (early-drinking and long-maturing). They sometimes lack fruit and generosity, but wines from the top growers recommended below are always the finest to be found. Ponsot • Jean Trapet Père & Fils
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault
- **Aging:** 10–20 years
- **Producers:** Drouhin-Laroze, Faiveley, Leroy B

#### LOUPIAC AOC

This appellation is located on the right bank of the Garonne, opposite Barsac. It is by far the best sweet-wine appellation in Entre-Deux-Mers and its wines are always excellent value.

- **White style:** Fine, viscous, honey-sweet wines that are lighter in body and color than Loupiac wines. Excellent value when they have rich botrytis character.
- **Grapes:** Sémillon, Sauvignon Blanc, Muscadelle; Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,
- **Aging:** 5–15 years (25 in exceptional cases); 4–8 years; 3–7 years

#### MÂCON AOC

Most wines from this district-wide appellation are produced in the area north of the Mâcon-Villages area.

- **Red style:** Better vinification techniques have improved these essentially Gamay wines, but Gamay is still a grape that does not like limestone.
- **White style:** These are basic-quality Chardonnay wines that are also fresh, frank, tasty, and dry; easy to drink and superb value. They may be sold as primeur or nouveau from the third Thursday of November following the harvest. ROSÉ These lightweight wines have an attractive, pale raspberry…
- **Grapes:** Gamay, Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 2–6 years; 1–4 years; 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Bénas Frères, E. Brocard (red), Desvignes

#### MÂCON SUPÉRIEUR AOC

All of France’s so-called “Supérieur” appellations merely demand extra alcohol (usually one degree more than the basic appellation), which does not necessarily make a superior wine.

- **Red style:** Aside from those listed, these wellcolored, medium-bodied wines are nothing special.
- **White style:** It is curious that almost a quarter of Mâcon Supérieur is white. The wines could just as easily sell as Mâcon AOC plain and simple. These wines may be sold as primeur or nouveau from the third Thursday of November following the harvest. ROSÉ These are attractively colored wines…
- **Grapes:** Gamay, Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 3–8 years; 1–4 years; 1–2 years
- **Producers:** Paul Beaudet (Terres Rouges), Père Tienne

This village has a good coopérative, and its wines a reputation for fatness and consistency. Blanc (d’Azenay) • des Grand Bruyères • Duvergey-Taboureau (de la Garonne)

This village is in an area that is also part of the Beaujolais-Villages and the similarly spelled Saint-Véran areas and its wines therefore have a choice of all three appellations.

More commonly seen than a few years ago, the wines from this village, which is adjacent to Chardonnay, are usually of a good, thirstquenching quality.

#### MÂCON-VERGISSON AOC

#### MÂCON-VERZÉ AOC

#### MÂCON-VILLAGES AOC

This white-only appellation covers 41 villages, 8 of which also fall within the Beaujolais-Villages appellation (see p.167); four of these have the additional right to the Saint-Véran AOC (see p.164).

- **White style:** These are some of the world’s most delicious, thirst-quenching, easy-drinking, dry Chardonnay wines. They also represent tremendous value. These wines may be sold as primeur or nouveau commencing from the third Thursday of November following the harvest. Gonon • René Michel…
- **Grapes:** Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 1–4 years
- **Producers:** Mâcon-Villages (without a named village), Jean Rijckeart

#### MÂCON-VINZELLES AOC

This village also has the right to PouillyVinzelles AOC and its wines therefore have a choice of two appellations.

#### MACVIN DU JURA AOC

Macvin, a vin de liqueur, is made by adding marc (local brandy distilled from the residue of skins) to grape juice, preventing fermentation.

- **Red style:** Typical dull vin de liqueur aroma, sweet and grapey.
- **White style:** This has a typical dull vin de liqueur aroma, and is sweet and grapey. This is the most oxidative Macvin style. ROSÉ Typical dull vin de liqueur aroma, sweet and grapey.
- **Grapes:** Poulsard, Trousseau, Pinot Noir; Chardonnay, Poulsard, Savagnin
- **Aging:** Upon purchase or never; Never!
- **Producers:** Labet

#### MADIRAN AOC


#### MARANGES AOC

In 1989 Maranges AOC replaced three separate appellations: Cheilly-lès-Maranges AOC, Dézizelès-Maranges AOC, and Sampigny-lès-Maranges AOC, a trio of villages sharing the once moderately famous cru of Marange, which is on a well-exposed hillside immediately southwest of Santenay.

- **Red style:** Wines with a very pure Pinot perfume, which are developing good color and body.
- **White style:** Rarely produced in any of the three villages and never in Dézize-lès-Maranges, as far as I am aware, although it is allowed. • René Martin • Edward Monnot • Claude Nouveau DE BEAUNE MARANGES
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault
- **Aging:** 2–7 years
- **Producers:** Fernand Chevrot, Contat-Grange, Jaffelin

#### MARSANNAY AOC

This village, situated in the very north of the Côte de Nuits, has long been famous for its rosé and has recently developed a reputation for its red wines.

- **Red style:** Firm and fruity wines with juicy redcurrant flavors, hints of liquorice, cinnamon and, if new oak has been used, vanilla.
- **White style:** Mostly light and tangy, early-drinking wines, although the top wines made in a suitable vintage, by the likes of Charles Audoin and Louis Jadot, are a lot richer, creamier and offer serious aging potential. ROSÉ These dry wines are rich, rather than light and fragrant. Packed…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 4-8 years; 2–5 years; 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Charles Audoin, René Bouvier, Bernard

#### MAZIS-CHAMBERTIN AOC

Grand Cru Sometimes known as Mazy-Chambertin. One of the nine grands crus of Gevrey-Chambertin.

- **Red style:** These complex wines have a stature second only to Chambertin and Clos de Bèze. They have a fine, bright color, supersilky finesse, and a delicate flavor that lasts. Esmonin • Faiveley • Jean-Michel Guillon • Lucien Lemoine • Leroy B • Bernard Maume • Philippe Nadeff • Joseph…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault
- **Aging:** 10–20 years
- **Producers:** Auvenay B, Bernard Dugat-Py, Frédéric

#### MAZOYÈRESCHAMBERTIN AOC

An alternative appellation for CharmesChambertin. See Charmes-Chambertin AOC.


#### MENETOU-SALON AOC

This underrated appellation covers MenetouSalon and the nine surrounding villages.

- **Red style:** These are dry, light-bodied, crisp, and fruity wines with fine varietal aroma. They are best drunk young, although some oak-matured examples can age well.
- **White style:** Bone-dry to dry wines. They are definitely Sauvignon in character, but the flavor can have an unexpected fragrance. ROSÉ Extremely good-quality, dry, light-bodied aromatic wines, full of straightforward fruit. Pellé
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir; Sauvignon Blanc
- **Aging:** 2–5 years; 1–2 years; Within 1 year
- **Producers:** Georges Chavet & Fils, De Loye, Henry

#### MERCUREY AOC

The wines of Mercurey, including the premiers crus, account for two-thirds of the production of the entire Côte Chalonnaise.

- **Red style:** Medium-bodied wines with excellent color and fine varietal character that have an exceptional quality-for-price ratio.
- **White style:** These delicious dry wines have a Chardonnay flavor that is more akin to that of the Côte de Beaune than it is to Mâconnais. Caves de Buxy • Château de Davenay (white) • Maurice Bertrand & François Juillot • Château de la Saule • Jean Vachet
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Pinot Chardonnay (sic); Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 5–12 years; 3–8 years; 4–12 years
- **Producers:** Brintet (white Vieilles Vignes), Château de, Stéphane Aladame, Arnoux Père & Fils

#### MERCUREY PREMIER CRU AOC

Premiers crus: La Bondue, Les Byots, La Cailloute, Champs Martins, La Chassière, Le Clos, Clos des Barraults, Clos Château de Montaigu, Clos l'Evêque, Clos des Myglands, Clos du Roi, Clos Tonnerre, Clos Voyens (or Les Voyens), Les Combins, Les Crêts, Les Croichots, Les Fourneaux 

- **Red style:** These premiers crus have increased in number from 5 to 27 and in area from 37 to over 250 acres (15 to 100 hectares).
- **White style:** I have encountered white Mercurey only at the basic village level. Juillot • Michel Juillot • Lorenzon • Guy Narjoux • François Raquillet • Antonin Rodet (Château de Chamirey)
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault
- **Aging:** 5–15 years
- **Producers:** Brintet, Faiveley, Jeannin-Nastet, Émile

#### MÉTHODE GAILLAÇOISE AOC

Sparkling wines made by the méthode rurale, involving just one fermentation, with no addition of a liqueur de tirage.

- **Sparkling style:** ROSÉ These wines are attractive, fresh, and deliciously fruity. Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc, Merlot GAILLAC MOUSSEUX MÉTHODE GAILLAÇOISE
- **Grapes:** At least 15% (each or in total) of Len de l’El; At least 60% Duras, plus Fer, Gamay, Syrah,
- **Aging:** 1–3 years; 1–2 years

#### MEURSAULT AOC

While the greatest white Côte de Beaune is either Montrachet or Corton-Charlemagne, Meursault is probably better known and is certainly more popular.

- **Red style:** This is often treated as a novelty, but it is a fine wine in its own right, with a firm edge.
- **White style:** Even the most basic Meursault should be deliciously dry with a nutty-buttery-spice quality added to its typically rich flavor. Père & Fils • Boyer-Martenot • Alain CocheBizouard • J.-F. Coche-Dury • Jean-Philippe Fichet • Henri Germain (Limozin) • Albert Grivault • Patrick…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 8–20 years; 5–12 years
- **Producers:** Robert Ampeau, d’Auvenay B, Bouchard

#### MEURSAULT-SANTENOTS AOC

This is an alternative appellation for Meursault Premier Cru that comes from a part of the Volnay-Santenots appellation. See VolnaySantenots AOC.

Cabernet Sauvignon and Cabernet Franc

#### MONTAGNEST.-ÉMILION AOC

This appellation includes St.-GeorgesSt-Émilion, a former commune that is today part of Montagne-St.-Émilion. St.-Georges-St.Émilion AOC and Montagne-St.-Émilion AOC are the best of all the appellations that append “St.-Émilion” to their names.

- **Red style:** Full, rich, and intensely flavored wines that mature well. Malbec, Merlot
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon,
- **Aging:** 5–15 years

#### MONTAGNY AOC

As all the vineyards in this AOC are premiers crus, the only wines that appear under the basic village appellation are those that fail to meet the technical requirement of 11.5 percent alcohol before chaptalization.

- **White style:** These dry white wines are good-value, fuller versions of the white Mâcon type. de Buxy • Faiveley • Louis Latour • Antonin Rodet MONTAGNY
- **Grapes:** Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 3–10 years
- **Producers:** Maurice Bertrand & François Juillot, Caves

#### MONTHÉLIE AOC

Monthélie’s wines, especially the premiers crus, are probably the most underrated in Burgundy. A few years ago there was a silly fuss over whether there should be an accent on the first “e” in Monthélie, and sure enough, a number of very authorative references differ.

- **Red style:** These excellent wines have a vivid color, expressive fruit, a firm structure, and a lingering, silky finish.
- **White style:** Relatively little white wine is produced. • J.-F. Coche-Dury • Paul Garaudet • Comte Lafon B • Monthélie-Douhairet • Annick Parent produce smooth, deliciously balanced wines that deserve more recognition. Jacques Germain • Olivier Leflaive MONTHÉLIE
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 7–15 years; 3–7 years; 4–8 years
- **Producers:** Eric Boigelot, Denis Boussey, Eric Boussey, Denis Père & Fils, P. Dubreuil-Fontaine

#### MONTHELIECÔTE DE BEAUNE AOC

Alternative appellation for red wines only. See Monthélie AOC.


#### MONTLOUIS-SUR-LOIRE AOC

All Montlouis appellations were renamed Montlouis-sur-Loire AOC from November 2003.

- **White style:** Light- to medium-bodied wines that can be dry or sweet. They are softer and more forward than the wines of Vouvray but can have the same honeyed flavor in fine years. Sweet Montlouis is aged in cask, but the best mediumdry styles are clean-fermented in stainless steel. for…
- **Grapes:** Chenin Blanc
- **Aging:** 1–3 years for medium-dry, up to 10 years
- **Producers:** Laurent Chatenay, François Chidaine, De

#### MONTRACHET AOC

Grand Cru Many consider Montrachet to be the greatest dry white wine in the world.

- **White style:** When it is fully mature, Montrachet has the most glorious and expressive character of all dry white wines. Its honeyed, toasty, floral, nutty, creamy, and spicy aromas are simply stunning. Bouchard Père & Fils • Chartron et Trebuchet • Marc Colin • Louis Jadot • Comte Lafon B •…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Chardonnay (sic)
- **Aging:** 10–30 years
- **Producers:** Guy Amiot & Fils, Amiot-Bonfils

#### MONTRAVEL AOC

The sweet version of this wine from the Pyrénées-Atlantiques was used at Henri de Navarre’s christening in 1553 and is often sold today as vendanges tardives or moelleux.

- **White style:** The best wines have a fine, spicy, and tangy bouquet and flavor, and can hint of pineapples and peaches, candied peel, and cinnamon. up to 15% (in total) of Camaralet and Lauzet Primo Palatum • Clos Thou • Clos Uroulat
- **Grapes:** Petit Manseng, Gros Manseng, Courbu, plus
- **Aging:** 5–20 years
- **Producers:** Bru-Baché, Cauhapé, Clos Lapeyre

#### MOREY-ST.-DENIS AOC

This excellent little wine village tends to be overlooked.

- **Red style:** The best of these village wines have a vivid color, a very expressive bouquet, and a smooth flavor with lots of finesse. A Morey-St.Denis from a domaine such as Dujac can have the quality of a top premier cru.
- **White style:** Dujac produces an excellent Morey-St.Denis blanc, but more interesting, although less consistent, is Ponsot’s Monts Luisants blanc. Although Monts Luisants is a premier cru, the upper section from which this wine comes is part of the village appellation (the southeastern corner…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 8–15 years; 3–8 years
- **Producers:** Pierre Amiot, Philippe Charlopin, Dujac

#### MORGON AOC

Cru Beaujolais Just as the Côte de Brouilly is a finer and more concentrated form of Brouilly, so the wines of Mont du Py in the center of Morgon are far more powerful than those of the surrounding vineyards in this commune.

- **Red style:** Although these wines are variable in character and quality, the best of them rank with those of Moulin-à-Vent as the most sturdy BARRELSANDFLOWERSATST. - AMOUR, BEAUJOLAIS Just five cru Beaujolais were originally classified in 1936 (Chénas, Chiroubles, Fleurie, Morgon, and…

#### MOULIN-À-VENT AOC

Cru Beaujolais The evocatively named Fleurie is the most expensive of the crus and its finest wines are the quintessence of classic Beaujolais.

- **Red style:** The wines of Fleurie quickly develop a fresh, floral, and fragrant style. Not as light and delicate as some writers suggest, their initial charm belies a positive structure and a depth of fruit that can sustain the wines for many years. produced in the very best vintages) (Cuvée…
- **Grapes:** Gamay
- **Aging:** 2–8 years (4–16 years for vin de garde styles

#### MOUSSEUX AOC

These are very consistent and undervalued generic traditional-method wines. The Savoie does not have a large-scale sparkling-wine industry as such, although it most certainly has the potential to build one.

- **Sparkling style:** WHITE These dry and delicately fruity wines have a fragrant aroma, fine acidity, and a good balance. Pinot Gris, Mondeuse Blanche, plus (in the Ain and Haute-Savoie départements) Chasselas, (in the Haute-Savoie and Isère départements) Molette, (in the Haute-Savoie département)…

#### MUSCAT DE MIREVAL AOC

This is a little-seen VDN appellation.

- **White style:** Light and sweet wines that can have a better balance and (relatively) more acidity than those from neighboring Frontignan. Mireval wines can have more elegance, although they may lack the raisiny-rich concentration. Neuf
- **Grapes:** Muscat Blanc à Petits Grains

#### MUSCAT DE RIVESALTES AOC

- **White style:** Musigny blanc is a rare and expensive dry wine produced solely at Comte Georges de Vogüé. It combines the steel of a Chablis with the richness of a Montrachet, although it never quite achieves the quality of either. J.-F. Mugnier (Château de ChambolleMusigny) • Christophe…

Grand Cru One of the six grands crus at the heart of Vosne-Romanée’s vineyards. Grand Cru This vineyard is owned by the Liger-Belair family, but the wine was matured, bottled, and sold by Bouchard Père & Fils until 2002, when Louis-Michel Liger-Belair took over.

#### SAINT-BRIS AOC

This wine is as good as most Sauvignon Blanc AOCs, and considerably better than many other white AOCs made from lesser grape varieties.

- **White style:** Fine wet-grass or herbaceous aromas, full smoky-Sauvignon flavors, and a correct, crisp, dry finish. Made from the rare Sauvignon Gris, Ghislaine & Jean-Hugues Goisot’s Cuvée du Corps de Garde Gourmand Fié Gris is the best, most consistent, and most unusual wine in this…

#### SAINT-VÉRAN AOC

This appellation overlaps the Mâconnais and Beaujolais districts, and is itself bisected by the Pouilly-Fuissé AOC, with two villages to the north (Davayé and Prissé) and five to the south (Chânes, Chasselas, Leynes, Saint-Amour, and Saint-Vérand).

- **White style:** Excellent value, fresh, dry, and fruity Chardonnay wines that are very much in the Mâcon-Villages style. Vincent, the proprietor of Château Fuissé, produces an amazingly rich wine that is far closer to Pouilly-Fuissé than to Macon-Villages, with hints of oak and honey. Pierre…
- **Grapes:** Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 1–4 years
- **Producers:** Daniel et Martine Barraud, Cordier

#### SANCERRE AOC

This appellation is famous for its white wines, although originally its reds were better known. Recently the reds and rosés have developed greater style.

- **Red style:** These wines have been more variable in quality than the whites, but the consistency is improving rapidly. They are dry, light- to medium-bodied wines, with a pretty floral aroma and a delicate flavor.
- **White style:** Classic Sancerre should be bone dry, highly aromatic, and have an intense flavor, sometimes tasting of gooseberries or even peaches in a great year. However, too many growers overproduce, never get the correct ripeness, and make the most miserable wines. ROSÉ Attractive, dry,…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir; Sauvignon Blanc
- **Aging:** 2–3 years; 1–3 years; Within 18 months
- **Producers:** Henri Bourgeois, Pascal Cotat, Lucien

#### SANTENAY AOC

This most southerly village appellation of the Côte d’Or (but not of the Côte de Beaune) is a source of good-value burgundy.

- **Red style:** These wines are fresh and frank, with a clean rendition of Pinot Noir fruit supported by a firm structure.
- **White style:** Only 2 percent of Santenay is white, but some good buys can be found among the top growers. Françoise & Denis Clair • Vincent Girardin • Alain Gras • Prieur-Brunet SANTENAY
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc

#### SAUMUR AOC

Saumur, situated within the borders of the Anjou appellation, is regarded as the pearl of Anjou. Its wine may be sold as Anjou, but Anjou does not automatically qualify as Saumur.

- **Red style:** These fine, bone-dry to dry, medium- to full-bodied wines are often similar to the red wines of Anjou, although they can vary from light and fruity to deep-colored and tannic. Pineau d’Aunis Collier • De Fiervaux • De ChâteauGaillard Z • Filliatreau • Guiberteau • Des Hautes…
- **White style:** Varying from bone-dry to sweet and from light- to full-bodied, these wines have a style more akin to Vouvray than Anjou, due to the limestone and the tufa soil. In poor-toaverage years, however, a Saumur is easily distinguished by its lighter body, leaner fruit, and a tartness…
- **Sparkling style:** ROSÉ An increasing number of pink Saumurs are pure Cabernet Franc and an increasing number are showing very well. However, the aggressive potential of this grape can quickly turn a thrilling raspberry-flavored fizz into something hideous. Pure Cabernet Sauvignon rosés can be…
