#### SAUMUR D’ORIGINE AOC

When the European Union banned the term méthode champenoise, this marketing term was developed by the producers of sparkling Saumur to promote and advertise their wines.


#### SAUMUR MOUSSEUX AOC

This is the technically correct appellation for all fully sparkling white and rosé Saumur wines made by the traditional method, but producers have shied away from the down-market term mousseux, selling the wines simply as Appellation Saumur Contrôlée.


#### SAUMUR-CHAMPIGNY AOC

Many people believe that the vineyards southeast of Saumur entitled to add the village name of Champigny to their appellation produce the best red wines in the Loire.

- **Red style:** Bone-dry to dry, full-bodied wines with a distinctive deep color and full and fragrant raspberry aromas, often tannic and long-lived. d’Aunis Des Champs Fleuris • Filliatreau • Château du Hureau (Lisagathe) • René-Noël Legrand • Petit Saint Vincent • De Rocfontaine (Vieilles…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon, Pineau
- **Aging:** 5–10 years
- **Producers:** Du Bois Moze Pasquier, La Bonnelière

#### SAUMURZ PÉTILLANT AOC

Little-used appellation for gently sparkling, traditional method wines with a minimum of nine months’ bottle age, which must be sold in ordinary still-wine bottles with regular corks.

- **Grapes:** A minimum of 80% Chenin Blanc and a
- **Aging:** Upon purchase

#### SAUSSIGNAC AOC

A sweet-wine appellation; it must have a minimum of 18 grams per liter of residual sugar. Any red wines are sold as Bergerac AOC.

- **White style:** The best can be very rich, fat, and full. Chenin Blanc (Flavie) • Château Grinou (Elevé en Fûts de Chêne) • Château La Maurigne (La Maurigne) • Château Miaudoux
- **Grapes:** Sémillon, Sauvignon Blanc, Muscadelle,
- **Aging:** 5–15 years
- **Producers:** Château Le Chabrier, Château Eyssards

#### SAUTERNES AOC

The much hillier communes of Bommes, Fargues, and Sauternes produce the richest of all Bordeaux’s dessert wines, while the châteaux in the lower-lying, flatter Preignac make wines very close in style to Barsac.

- **Red style:** Not big or bold wines with immediate appeal; the reds are elegant with some finesse. The fruit in recent vintages has tended to be a bit plumper, but it is in bottle that these wines take on richness, developing creamy-oak flavor. Merlot 25%, Cabernet Franc 6%, Malbec 5%, Petit…

#### SAVENNIÈRES AOC

When this small portion of Anjou Coteaux de la Loire produced only sweet wines, the AOC regulations set a correspondingly low maximum yield.

- **White style:** Bone-dry to dry wines of great mineral intensity, Savennières can be some of the longest-lived dry white wines in the world. Most critics believe that the single greatest Savennières is Nicolas Joly’s Clos de la Coulée de Serrant and, while I agree that it is one of the greatest…
- **Grapes:** Chenin Blanc
- **Aging:** 5–8 years (10–15 years for Clos de la Coulée
- **Producers:** Des Baumard, Du Closel (Clos du Papillon)

#### SAVENNIÈRES COULÉE-DE-SERRANT AOC

One of just two single-vineyard designations authorized for Savennières, Coulée-de-Serrant is 17 acres (7 hectares) and a mono-cru, solely owned by Nicolas Joly of Château de la Roche-aux-Moines.


#### SAVENNIÈRES ROCHE-AUX-MOINES AOC

The second and largest of the two single-vineyard designations authorized for Savennières, Roche-aux-Moines is 42 acres (17 hectares) and owned by three producers: Nicolas Joly of Château de la Roche-aux-Moines, Pierre and Yves Soulez of Château de Chamboureau, and Madame Laroche


#### SAVIGNY AOC

See Savigny-lès- Beaune AOC


#### SAVIGNY PREMIER CRU AOC

See Savigny-lès-Beaune Premier Cru AOC


#### SAVIGNY-LÈS-BEAUNE AOC

This village has gifted winemakers producing very underrated and undervalued wines.

- **Red style:** Delicious, easy-to-drink, medium-bodied wines that are very soft and Beaune-like in style.
- **White style:** Some excellent dry wines with good concentration of flavor, a smooth texture and some finesse, but they are difficult to find. Bouchard Père & Fils • Camus-Brochon • Philippe Delagrange (white) • Maurice Giboulot • Girard-Vollot • Pierre Guillemot • Lucien Jacob •…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 7–15 years; 4–10 years
- **Producers:** Robert Ampeau, Simon Bize & Fils

#### SAVIGNYCÔTE DE BEAUNE AOC

Alternative appellation for red wine only. See Savigny-lès-Beaune AOC.


#### SÉGURET AOC

Séguret produces red wine that is firm and fruity with a good, bright, deep color. A little white and rosé is also made but they seldom excel.


#### SEYSSEL AOC

This is a favorite après-ski wine.

- **White style:** These are fragrant, dry, refreshing wines.
- **Grapes:** Roussette
- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Maison Mollex

It was Varichon & Clerc that first carved a niche for Seyssel Mousseux in the export market.

Cru Beaujolais This is the most northerly of the 10 crus, and is more famous for its Mâcon wines than for its cru Beaujolais.

#### ST.-AUBIN AOC

This underrated village has many talented winemakers and is an excellent source for good-value wines.

- **Red style:** Delicious, ripe but light, fragrant, and fruity red wines that quickly develop a taste of wild strawberries.
- **White style:** Supervalue white wines—a sort of “Hautes-Côtes Montrachet”! Clair • Clerget • Marc Colin • Hubert Lamy & Fils
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 4–8 years; 3–8 years
- **Producers:** Jean-Claude Bachelet, Françoise & Denis

#### ST.-AUBIN PREMIER CRU AOC

One of two Montrachet villages producing some of the greatest dry whites in the world.

- **Red style:** Although some fine wines are made, Puligny-Montrachet rouge demands a premium for its scarcity.
- **White style:** Basic Puligny-Montrachet from a top grower is a very high-quality wine: full bodied, fine, and steely, requiring a few years to develop a nutty honey-and-toast flavor. Carillon & Fils • des Lambrays (Clos du Cailleret) • Leflaive B • Olivier Leflaive • Étienne Sauzet…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 10–20 years; 5–12 years
- **Producers:** Robert Ampeau, Jean-Marc Boillot, Louis

#### ST.-AUBINCÔTE DE BEAUNE AOC

Alternative appellation for red wines only. See St.-Aubin AOC. DE BEAUNE


#### ST.-CHINIAN AOC

Upgraded from VDQS in May 1982, the excellent-value appellation of St.-Chinian is the nearest that native southern French grapes come to resembling Bordeaux.

- **Red style:** Relatively light in color and weight, these wines have an elegance that belies their Mediterranean origin. Pelut, Mourvèdre, and Syrah, plus a maximum of 40% Carignan and no more than 30% Cinsault ROSÉ Dry and delicately fruity rosés with an attractive, fragrant bouquet and…
- **Grapes:** At least 50% in total of Grenache, Lladoner
- **Aging:** 2–6 years; 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Clos Bagatelle, Berloup (Vignes Royales)

#### ST.-ÉMILION AOC

These wines must have a minimum of 10.5 percent alcohol, but in years when chaptalization (the addition of sugar to grape juice to increase alcohol content) is allowed, there is also a maximum level of 13 percent.

- **Red style:** Even in the most basic St.-Émilions the ripe, spicy-juiciness of the Merlot grape should be supported by the firmness and finesse of the Cabernet Franc. The great châteaux achieve this superbly: they are full, rich, and concentrated, chocolaty and fruitcakey. Carmenère, Malbec,…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon,
- **Aging:** 6–12 years (modest growths);

#### ST.-GEORGESST.-ÉMILION AOC

Along with the Montagne region of Montagne-St-Émilion, this is the best parish of the outer areas.

- **Red style:** These are deep-colored, plummy wines with juicy, spicy fruit, and good supporting tannic structure. Cabernet Sauvignon, Malbec, Merlot
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc,
- **Aging:** 5–15 years

This is a commune with its own appellation in the northwest corner of Bourgueil. The soil is sandier than that of surrounding Bourgueil and the wines are lighter but certainly equal in terms of quality.

- **Red style:** Bone-dry to dry, medium-bodied wines that age well and have greater finesse than the wines of Bourgueil. Sauvignon ROSÉ A small amount of dry, medium-bodied rosé with firm, fruity flavor is produced. Sauvignon Malagnes) • Sebastian David • Frédéric Malibeau (Coutures, Eclipse) •…

#### ST.-PÉRAY AOC

This white-wine-only village appellation is curiously out of step with the rest of the area.

- **White style:** Firm and fruity, with good acidity for a wine from the south of the region, but unfortunately it usually lacks charm. Even the best growers within this region, recommended below, struggle to produce anything of interest. Lionnet • Alain Voge
- **Grapes:** Marsanne, Roussanne
- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** J.-F. Chaboud, Bernard Gripa, Jean

#### ST.-PÉRAY MOUSSEUX AOC

This is a traditional method sparkling wine made from the wrong grapes grown on the wrong soil. The growers would be advised to rip the whole lot up and replant with black grapes for a red Côtes du Rhône.

- **Sparkling style:** WHITE An overrated, dry sparkling wine with a coarse mousse.
- **Grapes:** Marsanne, Roussanne

#### ST.-ROMAIN AOC

A little village amid picturesque surroundings in the hills above Auxey-Duresses.

- **Red style:** Good-value, medium-bodied, rustic reds that have a good, characterful flavor.
- **White style:** Fresh and lively, light- to mediumbodied dry white wines of an honest Chardonnay style. Joseph Drouhin • Alain Gras • ThéveninMonthélie
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 4–8 years; 3–7 years
- **Producers:** Ambroise Bertrand, de Chassorney

#### TOURAINE MESLAND AOC

Wines from the vineyards of Mesland and the five surrounding villages on the right bank of the Loire. The reds and rosés of this appellation are definitely well worth looking out for.

- **Red style:** These dry, medium- to full-bodied wines are the best of the AOC and can be as good as those of Chinon or Bourgueil. Cabernet Franc and Malbec, with an optional maximum of 10% Cabernet Sauvignon until 2010, when it will no longer be allowed
- **White style:** Dry, light-bodied wines with a high acidity that is only tamed in the best and sunniest years. maximum of 30% Sauvignon Blanc, and a maximum of 15% Chardonnay ROSÉ These dry, medium-bodied wines have more depth and character than those of Touraine Amboise. Franc and Malbec
- **Grapes:** A minimum of 60% Gamay, plus 10–30%; A minimum of 60% Chenin Blanc, plus a; A minimum of 80% Gamay, plus Cabernet
- **Aging:** 1–3 years; 1–2 years
- **Producers:** De la Briderie Z, Château Gaillard

#### TOURAINE MOUSSEUX AOC

Very good-value traditional method red, white, and rosé wines. While the grapes for the white and rosé can come from the entire Touraine AOC area, those for red Touraine Mousseux may come from only the following areas: Bourgueil, St.-Nicolas-de-Bourgueil, or Chinon.

#### VIN DE PAILLE AOC

These wines are made from grapes that are dried to concentrate the juice, after which fermentation is long and the wine is given up to four years in wood.

- **White style:** These very sweet wines have old-gold, honey-gold, and amber-gold colors; a full, distinctive, and complex bouquet; a powerfully rich and nutty flavor; and a surprisingly crisp finish, with a raisiny, apricotskin aftertaste. Richard Delay • Château Gréa • Labet • Morel-Thibaut •…
- **Grapes:** Poulsard, Trousseau, Savagnin, Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 10–50 (or more) years
- **Producers:** Berthet-Bondet, Denis & Marie Chevassu, Richard Delay, Grand Frères (Prestige)

#### VIN DE SAVOIE AOC

- **White style:** These dry wines are the best of the AOC, with Abymes, Apremont, and Chignin the best villages. All are fine, rich, and complex. Blanche, Roussette, Veltliner Précoce, plus a maximum of 10% (in the Ain and HauteSavoie départements) of Chasselas, Gringet, Roussette d’Ayze, and (in…

- **Aging:** 2–8 years; 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Pierre Boniface, Dupasquier, De L’Idylle

#### VIN JAUNE AOC

For details of production and style, see Château-Chalon AOC.

- **Producers:** Château d’Arlay, Philippe Butin

#### VINEUSE AOC

These underrated wines of Mâcon-La Roche Vineuse are produced on west- and south-facing slopes north of Pouilly-Fuissé, the potential of which is pushed to the very limits by Olivier Merlin, one of Mâcon’s greatest winemakers.

- **Producers:** Olivier Merlin, Groupement de Producteurs

#### VINS DE L’ORLÉANAIS VDQS

These wines have been made for centuries, but only one-third of the appellation is worked now.

- **Red style:** Dry, medium-bodied, fresh, and fruity wines that are given a short maceration, producing a surprisingly soft texture. They are usually sold as pure varietal wines: the Pinot can be delicate, the Cabernet Franc is fuller.
- **White style:** Very small quantities of interesting wines are made from Chardonnay, known locally as Auvernat Blanc. Dry, medium-bodied, and surprisingly smooth and fruity. Gris (syn. Auvernat Gris) ROSÉ The local speciality is a dry, light- to medium-bodied rosé known as Meunier Gris— an…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Meunier, Cabernet Franc; Chardonnay (syn. Auvernat Blanc), Pinot
- **Aging:** 1–2 years; Within 1 year
- **Producers:** Clos St.-Fiacre, Vignerons de la

#### VINS DU THOUARSAIS VDQS

Michel Gigon is the sole producer of Vins du Thouarsais, a wine that could once boast over a hundred growers.

- **Red style:** Dry, light- to medium-bodied, fruity reds, sometimes reminiscent of cherries and other stone fruit.
- **White style:** A lighter-bodied but far more fragrant version of dry and semisweet Anjou Blanc. ROSÉ This is a splendid dry, light-bodied or light- to medium-bodied picnic wine. THELOIREVALLEY - TOURAINE TOURAINE Wines bearing Touraine appellations are prolific and capable of giving good…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon, Gamay; Chenin Blanc plus up to 20% Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 1–2 years; Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Michel Gigon

#### VINSOBRES AOC

Only the red wine of Rochegude may claim the Côtes du Rhône Villages appellation. I have encountered only the local coopérative wine, but it is good quality, well colored, soft, and plummy.

- **Producers:** C. V. de Rochegude

#### VIRÉ-CLESSÉ AOC

This appellation was created in February 1999, by combining the Mâcon-Viré and Mâcon-Clessé subappellations, and recognizing the wines from Viré-Clessé in their own right, above and beyond those of Mâcon-Villages.

- **Grapes:** Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 1–4 years
- **Producers:** All those recommended under Mâcon-Viré

#### VISAN AOC

These red wines have good color and true vin de garde character. Fresh, quaffing white wines are also made. tde la Coste Chaude • Olivier Cuilleras CÔTES DU


#### VIVARAIS AOC

The côtes of Vivarais look across the Rhône River to the coteaux of Tricastin. Its best cru villages (Orgnac, St.-Montant, and St.-Remèze) may add their names to the Côtes du Vivarais appellation, and of these, the wines of the village of Orgnac stand out.

- **Red style:** These light, quaffing reds are by far the best wines in the district. Carignan have been banned, and this wine must now contain a minimum of 90% (in total) of Syrah and Grenache; the Syrah must account for at least 40% and Grenache at least 30%, with Cinsault and Carignan…
- **Grapes:** Since 1995 Mourvèdre, Picpoul, Aubun, and
- **Aging:** 1–3 years

#### VOLNAY AOC

Volnay ranks in performance with such great crus as Gevrey-Chambertin and ChambolleMusigny. It is the most southerly red-wine-only appellation in the Côte d’Or, and the only great wine village located above its vineyards.

- **Red style:** These wines are not cheap, but they are firm and well colored with more silky finesse than should be expected from a village appellation. Régis Rossignol
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris, Pinot Liébault
- **Aging:** 6–15 years
- **Producers:** Marquis d’Angerville, Michel Lafarge

- **Producers:** Marquis d’Angerville, Jean-Marc Boillot

#### VOSNE-ROMANÉE AOC

This is the most southerly of the great villages of the Côte de Nuits. Some Vosne-Romanée vineyards are in neighboring Flagey-Echézeaux.

- **Red style:** Sleek, stylish, medium-bodied wines of the purest Pinot Noir character and with the silky texture so typical of the wines of this village. Clavelier • Engel • Confuron-Contédidot • Bernard Dugat-Py (Vieilles Vignes) • Forey Père & Fils • Jean Grivot • Anne Gros • Michel Gros •…

The modest village wines of Vougeot are a relative rarity, this appellation covering less than 12 acres (5 hectares), which is less than onetenth of the area encompassed by the Clos de Vougeot grand cru itself.

#### BOURGOGNE CHITRY AOC

A true single-village appellation, in that only vines in Chitry qualify. This village neighbors St.-Bris-le-Vineux to the northeast, and its vineyards are comprised of 67 acres (27 hectares) of black grapes, and 67 acres (27 hectares) of white grapes.

- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Liébault, Pinot Gris, César,
- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Patrick & Christine Chalmeau (blanc only)

#### BOURGOGNE COULANGESLA-VINEUSE AOC

The far-flung borders of Coulanges-la-Vineuse encompass no fewer than six communes in addition to its own: Charentenay, EscolivesSainte-Camille, Migé, Mouffy, Jussy, and Val-deMercy.

- **Red style:** Most of these wines are primarily Pinot Noir with a small dash of César. Most are simply frank and fruity, although the best can be quite rich, with truly expressive fruit. César, Tressot
- **White style:** Not worth buying. ROSÉ Not tasted. César, Tressot Houblin (rouge only) • Jean-Pierre Maltoff (rouge only) • Alain Rigoutat (rouge only) BOURGOGNE

#### BOURGOGNE VÉZELAY AOC

This appellation southeast of the main Chablis district covers some 158 acres (64 hectares) of vines on the steep upper slopes (above the frost-line) overlooking the village of Vézelay itself.

- **White style:** At its most basic level, not worth buying, but top-performing white wines from these appellations are superior to the lower end of Chablis, which is relatively much more expensive. They are invariably pure Chardonnay, yet rarely taste like Chablis, being softer and smoother.…
- **Grapes:** Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 1–3 years

#### CHABLIS AOC

With careful selection, basic Chablis can be a source of tremendous-value, classic 100 percent Chardonnay wine, particularly in the best vintages.

- **White style:** Always totally dry, the grands crus are the biggest, richest, and most complex of all Chablis. Many show new oak characteristics that may unfortunately dominate the wine. Their individual styles depend very much on how the winemaker vinifies and matures the wine, but when well…
- **Grapes:** Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 2–6 years
- **Producers:** Christian Adine, Baillard, Billaud-Simon

#### D’AUXERRE AOC

Overlapping the Saint-Bris appellation, Côte d’Auxerre covers various parcels of vines scattered throughout the hillsides overlooking Augy, Auxerre-Vaux, Quenne, St.-Bris-leVineux, and in part of Vincelottes that does not qualify for Irancy.

- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Bailly-Lapierre, Bersan, Patrice Fort

#### ÉPINEUIL AOC

A true single-village appellation, Épineuil consists of 210 acres (85 hectares) of hillside vineyards on the banks of the Amançon River, overlooking Tonnerre, northeast of Chablis.

- **Red style:** Most are light and undistinguished, but the Domaine de l’Abbaye du Petit Quincy makes a much darker version, especially its Côte de Grisey and Côte de Grisey Cuvée Juliette, which many consider to be the finest wines of Épineuil. Neither light nor dark, the ruby-colored wine…
- **White style:** Few wines deliver more than a straightforward fresh, crisp, dry white of fairly neutral character. ROSÉ At its best, this can be a deliciously fresh, easy-drinking rosé. César, Tressot Eric Dampt (white only) • Dominique Gruhier • Alain Mathias • de Chantemerle • Jean Collet •…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Pinot Liébault, Pinot Gris,; Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc
- **Aging:** 1–5 years; Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Abbaye du Petit Quincy, La Chablisienne

### BEAUJOLAIS

#### BEAUJOLAIS SUPÉRIEUR AOC

Only 1 percent of all Beaujolais wines carry this appellation.

- **Red style:** By no means superior to Beaujolais AOC—buy basic Beaujolais for fun or cru Beaujolais for more serious drinking.
- **White style:** Barely 5 percent of this tiny appellation produces white wine. Fine as it may be, it has no intrinsic superiority over the quaffing quality of basic Beaujolais blanc. ROSÉ I have not encountered any pink versions of this appellation. Saint-Vérand
- **Grapes:** Gamay, Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris; Chardonnay, Aligoté
- **Aging:** 3–8 years; 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Cave Beaujolais du Bois-d’Oingt, Cave

#### BEAUJOLAISVILLAGES AOC

The 38 villages that may add their names to the Beaujolais AOC (see Beaujolais [village name] AOC) also have the right to this appellation and must use it if the wine is a blend of wines from two or more villages.

- **Red style:** If you find a good example, these wines are well coloured with a rich Gamay flavor and should have all the superiority that Beaujolais Supérieur wines mysteriously lack.
- **White style:** Very little encountered, but more Villages blanc is produced than Beaujolais blanc. These wines may be sold as primeur or nouveau from the third Thursday of November following the harvest. ROSÉ Seldom encountered, but the Cave Beaujolais du Bois-d’Oingt makes an attractive wine.…
- **Grapes:** Gamay, Pinot Noir, Pinot Gris; Chardonnay, Aligoté
- **Aging:** 3–8 years; 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Accoeur, Philippe Deschamps, des

#### CÔTES DE TOUL AOC

Part of the once-flourishing vineyards of Lorraine, these côtes are located in eight communes west of Toul, in the département of Meurthe-et-Moselle, and were elevated to AOC status in 2003.

- **Red style:** The Pinot Noir is the most successful, and the wine is usually sold as a pure varietal. It can have surprisingly good color for wine from such a northerly region, and good cherry-Pinot character.
- **White style:** These wines represent less than two percent of the VDQS, just 844 cases (76 hectoliters). Nevertheless, the Auxerrois is the best grape, its fatness making it ideal for such a northerly area with a calcareous soil. ROSÉ Most Côtes de Toul is made and sold as vin gris. This pale…

- **Producers:** Vincent Gorny, Michel & Marcel Laroppe, René Barth, Bernard Becht, Joseph Gruss

#### CRÉMANT D’ALSACE AOC

Although small growers like Dirler had made Vin Mousseux d’Alsace as early as 1880, it was not until 1900 that Dopff Au Moulin created a sparkling wine industry on a commercial scale, and 1976 before an AOC was established.

Its voluptuous, upfront style…
- **Grapes:** Gewurztraminer
- **Aging:** 3–10 years (20–30 years for great examples)
- **Producers:** Barmès-Buecher B, Charles Baur B

#### MOSELLE VDQS

This VDQS was called Vins de Moselle until 1995, when it became simply Moselle. Although many restaurants list German Mosel as “Moselle,” the river and the wine it produces are called Mosel in Germany, only becoming Moselle when crossing the border into France (and Luxembourg).

- **Red style:** Château de Vaux produces a surprisingly good Pinot Noir.

#### MUSCAT AOC

The best Muscat wine, some growers believe, is made from the Muscat d’Alsace, a synonym for both the white and pink strains of the rich, full Muscat à Petits Grains.

- **White style:** Dry, aromatic wines with fine floral characteristics that often smell of orange-flower water and taste of peaches. A top-quality Muscat that is expressive of its terroir is a great wine. Petits Grains, Muscat Ottonel (Clos St.-Imer) • Pierre Henri Ginglinger B • Roger Jung •…
- **Grapes:** Muscat Blanc à Petits Grains, Muscat Rosé à
- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Jean Becker, Emile Beyer, Ernest Burn

#### PINOT AOC


#### PINOT BLANC AOC

This designation should only be used if the wine is made from 100 percent Pinot Blanc. See also Pinot AOC


#### PINOT GRIS AOC


#### PINOT NOIR AOC

Not so long ago, Pinot Noir d’Alsace was synonymous in style with rosé, but the trend has swung hard over toward a true red wine.

- **Red style:** Most are unsatisfactory (overextracted, too tannic, or too oaky—sometimes all three), but Marcel Deiss’s Burlenberg is in a different class and is comparable to a good Burgundy. A step down, but still good, is Domaine Weinbach Réserve in a good year, and the most commercial of…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir
- **Aging:** 2–6 years (12 years for exceptional cuvées); 1–2 years
- **Producers:** Marcel Deiss B (Burlenberg), Hugel & Fils

#### RIESLING AOC

Of all Alsace grape varieties, Riesling is the most susceptible to differences in soil: clay soils give fatness and richness; granite Riesling is also rich, but with more finesse; limestone adds obvious finesse but less richness; and volcanic soil gives a well-flavored, spicy sty

- **White style:** In youth, fine Rieslings can show hints of apple, fennel, citrus, and peach, but can be so firm and austere that they give no hint of the beautiful wines into which they can evolve. Barmès-Buecher B • Léon Baur B • Léon Beyer • Paul Blanck Ö (Furstentum, Schlossberg) • Albert…
- **Grapes:** Riesling
- **Aging:** 4–20 years
- **Producers:** J B Adam, Lucien Albrecht (Clos Schild)

#### SYLVANER AOC

Hugh Johnson once described the Sylvaner as “local tap-wine,” and this is how it should be served—direct from the stainless-steel vat, with all the zip and zing of natural carbonic gas (normally filtered out during the bottling process).

- **White style:** Sylvaner is an unpretentious, dry, lightto medium-bodied wine, with fragrance rather than fruitiness. It is generally best drunk young, but, like the Muscat, exceptionally long-living examples can always be found. Rolly Gassmann • Weinbach/Faller • ZindHumbrecht B
- **Grapes:** Sylvaner
- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Agathe Bursin, JosMeyer B (Rouge—sic)

#### VIN D’ALSACE AOC

Alternative designation for Alsace AOC.


### LOIRE VALLEY

#### CHÂTEAUMEILLANT VDQS

Borders the Cher and Indre, around Bourges. Although only red-wine grapes are grown, white wine of some standing used to be made here, and its production is still permitted.

- **Red style:** The Gamay grape usually dominates the red wines, which should be drunk as young as possible. ROSÉ The best Châteaumeillant wines. They are fresh, grapey, and delicately balanced. et Frédéric Dallot • De Pavillon
- **Grapes:** Gamay, Pinot Gris, Pinot Noir
- **Aging:** 6–12 months
- **Producers:** Du Chaillot, CV Châteaumeillant, Valérie

#### CÔTE ROANNAISE AOC

Red and rosé wines made from a localized Gamay clone called Gamay Saint-Romain. Grown on south- and southwest-facing slopes of volcanic soil on the left bank of the Loire some 25 miles (40 kilometers) west of the Mâconnais district of Burgundy.

- **Red style:** Some of these dry, medium- to full-bodied wines are produced using a form of macération carbonique, and a few are given a little maturation in cask. The result can vary between well-colored wines that are firm and distinctive, and oaky and fruity Beaujolais-type versions for…
- **Grapes:** Gamay
- **Aging:** 1–5 years; 2–3 years
- **Producers:** Alain Baillon (Cuvée Monplaisir), Paul et

#### COTEAUX D’ANCENIS VDQS

These varietal VDQS wines, which come from the same area as Muscadet Coteaux de la Loire, should be promoted to full AOC status.

- **Red style:** Bone-dry to dry, and light- to mediumbodied wines that include Cabernets, made from both Cabernet Franc and Cabernet Sauvignon grapes. Surprisingly, they are not as successful as the juicy Gamay wines that represent no less than 80 percent of the total production of this…
- **White style:** Dry to medium-dry, light-bodied wines. The Pinot Gris, also sold as “Malvoisie,” is not as alcoholic as its Alsatian cousin, yet can possess a light richness that will linger in the mouth. The Chenin Blanc, known locally as “Pineau de la Loire,” is rarely very special. ROSÉ…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Sauvignon, Cabernet Franc,; Chenin Blanc, Pinot Gris
- **Aging:** 2 years; 12–18 months
- **Producers:** CV des Terroirs de la Noëlle, Guindon

#### COTEAUX DE LA LOIRE AOC

The Coteaux de la Loire is the most northerly wine area on the French coast, above which it is almost impossible to grow grapes of sufficient ripeness for winemaking.

- **White style:** Bone-dry, light-bodied wines of variable quality, usually lacking in fruit, but can be the best balanced of all Muscadets in very hot years. Varenne MUSCADET CÔTES DE
- **Grapes:** Muscadet
- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Guindon, Du Moulin Giron, De la

#### CÔTES D’AUVERGNE VDQS

Côtes d’Auvergne is located south of SaintPourçain and west of Côtes du Forez on the edge of the Massif Central. This is the most remotely situated of all the outer areas that fall officially within the Loire region.

- **Red style:** The best of these dry, light-bodied, and fruity wines carry the Chanturgues appellation. Most are made from Gamay, a grape that has traditionally been grown in the area, and are very much in the style of Beaujolais.
- **White style:** These dry, light-bodied wines made from Chardonnay have been overlooked but can be more accessible than the same variety grown in more classic areas of the Loire, thus they are surely a marketable commodity in view of Chardonnay’s upmarket status. ROSÉ These are dry,…
- **Grapes:** Gamay, Pinot Noir; Chardonnay
- **Aging:** 1–2 years; Within 1 year
- **Producers:** Vignoble de l’Arbe Blanc, Pradier, Gilles

#### CÔTES DU FOREZ AOC

Similar to Beaujolais, this wine is produced in the Loire département adjacent to Lyon, and was upgraded from VDQS to AOC in 2000.

- **Red style:** These are dry, light-bodied wines with some fruit. They are best drunk young and at a cool temperature. ROSÉ Simple, light-bodied, dry rosés that make attractive, unpretentious picnic wines. Foreziens • Odile Verdier et Jacky Logel
- **Grapes:** Gamay
- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Gilles Bonnefoy (La Madone), Les Vignerons

#### DE LOIRE AOC

Of all the Loire’s sparkling wines, this is probably the most underrated, yet it has the greatest potential because it can be blended from the wines of Anjou-Saumur and Touraine and from the widest range of grape varieties.

- **Sparkling style:** ROSÉ The best of these light- to medium-bodied wines are brut, and usually contain a high proportion of Cabernet Franc and Grolleau Noir grapes. Cabernet Franc makes the most distinctive wine. A pure Pinot Noir crémant rosé would be interesting to taste. Sauvignon, Pineau…
- **Grapes:** Chenin Blanc, Cabernet Franc, Cabernet
- **Aging:** 1–3 years; Most are best consumed upon purchase,
- **Producers:** Baumard, Yves Lambert, Château de

#### FIEFS VENDÉENS VDQS

A vin de pays until 1984, this appellation has been steadily improving and deserves its VDQS status. The regulations controlling the grape varieties permitted for this appellation are unique.

- **Red style:** The communes of Vix and Mareuil-sur-LayDisais produce the best wines. They are dry, medium-bodied, and firm, but not long-lived. They can have a grassy character, derived from the Cabernet Franc, which is the predominant grape grown in both these villages. plus Cabernet Franc,…
- **White style:** Bone-dry to dry, light-bodied wines which, apart from those of Vix and Pissotte, are of limited quality. This could be because the Chenin Blanc rarely ripens properly in a northerly coastal area. If some of the other permitted grape varieties were grown over a much wider area,…
- **Grapes:** A minimum of 50% Gamay and Pinot Noir; A minimum of 50% Chenin Blanc, plus
- **Aging:** Within 18 months; Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Coirer (Pissotte Sélection), Des Dames (Les

#### GRANDLIEU AOC

Delimited as recently as 1994, this area west of Sèvre-et-Maine once represented 73 percent of the basic Muscadet appellation.

- **White style:** Bone-dry, light-bodied wines that initially displayed considerable variation in quality, but the best are now showing a fine, floral-minerality of fruit. Hautes Noëlles MUSCADET DE
- **Grapes:** Muscadet
- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Du Fief Guerin, Gandais Père & Fils, Les

#### GROS PLANT NANTAIS VDQS

Gros Plant is the local synonym for the Folle Blanche—one of the grapes used to make Cognac.

- **White style:** Gros Plant is normally so dry, tart, and devoid of fruit and body that it seems tough and sinewy to taste. I would rather drink lemon juice than 99 percent of the Gros Plant that I have had, but if yields are limited and the wine bottled sur lie, it can have sufficient depth to…
- **Grapes:** Gros Plant
- **Aging:** Usually upon purchase
- **Producers:** De la Boitaudière (Sur Lie), Guy Bossard Z

#### HAUT-POITOU VDQS

Situated 50 miles (80 kilometers) southwest of Tours, the Poitiers district produces wines that have achieved a remarkable reputation despite its hot, dry climate, and flat land that is more suited to arable farming than to viticulture.

- **Red style:** This is a wine to watch: although the really successful reds have until now been in short supply and confined mainly to Cabernet, there is a feeling that a general breakthrough is imminent. The quality of the entire appellation is likely to rise significantly, and there is…
- **White style:** Dry, light- to medium-bodied, varietal wines. Those made from pure Sauvignon are softer and more floral than most of their northern counterparts, yet retain the freshness and vitality that is so important to this grape variety. and up to a maximum of 20% Chenin Blanc ROSÉ These…
- **Grapes:** Pinot Noir, Gamay, Merlot, Malbec, Cabernet; Sauvignon Blanc, Chardonnay, Pinot Blanc,
- **Aging:** Within 3 years; Within 1 year
- **Producers:** Ampelidæ, Du Centaure, C. V. Haut-Poitou

#### MUSCADET AOC

This basic appellation covers the whole Muscadet area, yet the wines produced under it account for only 10 percent of the total production.

- **White style:** Bone-dry, light-bodied wines that, with very few exceptions, are ordinary at best, and often lack balance. Not one basic Muscadet has impressed me in recent years. These wines may be sold as primeur or nouveau as from the third Thursday of November following the harvest.…
- **Grapes:** Muscadet
- **Aging:** Upon purchase

#### MUSCADET SUR LIE AOC

Until recently there were no controls and unscrupulous producers would simply describe an ordinary filtered wine as sur lie and thereby demand a higher price.

- **Aging:** eeping a wine in contact with its lees in huge

#### ORLÉANS VDQS

Although made an appellation as recently as 2000, vines have been growing in this area since the 10th century. Legend has it that vines were originally brought from the Auvergne, hence the local synonyms of Auvernat Rouge for Pinot Noir and Auvernat Blanc for Chardonnay.

- **Red style:** Successful wines have good color and just about enough fruit to balance the tannins, affording a more supple finish. Montigny-Piel’s Clos Saint-Fiacre stands out as the best wine of this appellation in all its color formats. Noir and Pinot Gris
- **White style:** The least exciting wines so far, although it is very early days for these dry white wines, which must possess no more than 4 grams per liter of residual sugar. Pinot Gris ROSÉ The few encountered have been fresh, easy drinking with Meunier-cum-Pinot character, expressed more as…
- **Grapes:** Minimum of 60% Pinot Meunier, plus Pinot; Minimum of 60% Chardonnay, plus
- **Aging:** Within 3 years; Within 1 year
- **Producers:** Montigny-Piel (Clos Saint-Fiacre)

#### ORLÉANS-CLÉRY VDQS

This subappellation was granted in 2002, and is restricted to red wines produced in Cléry-SaintAndré and four neighboring villages southwest of Orléans, but confusingly from entirely different grapes from the main appellation.

- **Red style:** The local coopérative (Grand Maison) makes a good wine from 25-year-old vines, which is old for this rediscovered part of the Loire, but most Cabernet-based wines from this area suffer too much from a herbaceous character. Cabernet Sauvignon planted before October 2002
- **Grapes:** Minimum of 75% Cabernet Franc, plus
- **Aging:** Within 3 years
- **Producers:** Jacky Legroux, C. V. de la Grand Maison

#### PINEAU DES CHARENTES AOC

A vin de liqueur produced in the Cognac region, which is between the Loire and Bordeaux (thus not really the Loire Valley, but it has to go somewhere!).

- **White style:** Few manage to rise above the typically dull, oxidative vin de liqueur style that goes rancio with age. Colombard, Folle Blanche, Jurançon Blanc, Meslier Saint-François, Merlot, Merlot Blanc, Montils, Sauvignon Blanc, Sémillon, Ugni Blanc ROSÉ Few manage to rise above the…
- **Grapes:** Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon,
- **Aging:** Upon opening
- **Producers:** Barbeau & Fils (Tres Vieux Rosé Grand

#### ROSÉ DE LOIRE AOC

A dry rosé wine introduced in 1974 to exploit the international marketing success of Rosé d’Anjou and to take advantage of the trend for drier styles.

- **Grapes:** Pineau d’Aunis, Pinot Noir, Gamay,
- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** De Chanteloup, Lecheteau, De Montgilet

#### SÈVRE-ET-MAINE AOC

Classic Muscadet from a small area containing most of the best wines. Some 45 percent of this appellation is bottled and sold as sur lie, having remained in contact with its sediment for at least one winter before bottling.

- **White style:** Bone-dry to dry, light-bodied wines. The best should have fruit, acidity, and elegance, but although they can be reminiscent of a modest white Burgundy and exceptional wines can survive considerable aging, they seldom improve and always trade off finesse for depth. • Michel…
- **Grapes:** Muscadet
- **Aging:** 2 years, although some may last 3–4 years
- **Producers:** Guy Bossard Z (De l’Ecu), De la Braudière

#### ST.-POURÇAIN VDQS

The wines of Saint-Pourçain have a long and impressive history for what is, after all, an obscure VDQS, with vineyards first planted not by the Romans, as elsewhere in the Loire Valley, but by the Phoenicians.

- **Red style:** Dry, light- to medium-bodied wines, but they can vary from very light Beaujolais look-alikes to imitations of Bourgogne Passetoutgrains, depending on the grape varieties in the blend. of 10% Gamay Teinturier
- **White style:** Dry, light- to medium-bodied wines. The Tresallier grape (which is known as the Sacy in Chablis), when blended with Chardonnay and Sauvignon, produces a crisp, toasty, full-flavored wine that does have some merit. Very little is known about the SaintPierre-Doré, except that…
- **Grapes:** Gamay, Pinot Noir, and up to a maximum; A maximum of 50% Tresallier and 10%
- **Aging:** 1–2 years
- **Producers:** De Bellevue, Jallet (Les Ceps Centenaires)

#### ANCESTRALE AOC

CLAIRETTE DU LANGUEDOC “RANCIO” AOC Formerly called Vin de Blanquette, but still produced by the ancient méthode rurale, this wine was apparently invented by the monks at the abbey of St.-Hilaire in 1531.

- **Sparkling style:** WHITE These succulently sweet sparkling wines are a hedonist’s dream and should be far more commercially available. The ancient méthode rurale should be perfected so that prestige cuvées can be sold at a premium, See Clairette du Languedoc AOC

#### BANYULS AOC

The most southerly appellation in France, the vineyards of this vin doux naturel are literally a stone’s throw from those of Spain.

- **Red style:** This is the deepest and darkest of all VDNs. A rich, sweet, red Banyuls (without too much barrel-age) has a chocolaty, bottledfruitiness, which is the nearest France gets to the great wines of Portugal’s Douro region. It lacks the fire of a great port, but has its own immense…
- **White style:** /ROSÉ/TAWNY Like all VDNs that may be made in red, white, and rosé style, they can all turn tawny with time, particularly “rancio” wines.
- **Aging:** 10–40 years

#### BANYULS GRAND CRU AOC

The requirements are the same as for Banyuls AOC, but a minimum of 75 percent Grenache is required. The grapes must be destemmed and macerated for a minimum of five days and the wine matured in oak for at least 30 months.

- **Producers:** L’Abbé Rous (Christian Reynal), L’Étoile

#### CABARDÈS AOC

See Banyuls AOC An obscure appellation north of Carcassonne, these wines were once sold as Côtes du Cabardès et de l’Orbiel VDQS, but this hardly tripped off the tongue, thus the shortened form was in common usage long before the appellation was promoted to full AOC status in 199

- **Red style:** The best wines have elegant fruit and a leaner, more Bordeaux-like balance than most of the warmer, spicy-ripe southern French reds. minimum of 40% Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon, and Merlot (Aubun and Carignan are no longer allowed) ROSÉ A rich, fruity, well-colored rosé.…
- **Grapes:** At least 40% Grenache and Syrah, plus a
- **Aging:** 3–8 years; Between 2–3 years
- **Producers:** Château de La Bastide (L’Esprit), Claude et

#### CABRIÈRES AOC

This is a single commune of steep schistous slopes in the center of the Clairette du Languedoc subappellation. Cabrières’s production is dominated by the local coopérative.

- **Producers:** Du Temple (Jacques de Molay)

#### COLLIOURE AOC

An obscure but exciting appellation for unfortified wines from normally harvested grapes grown in Banyuls, the eponymous wine of which is made from only the ripest, late-picked (but not botrytized) grapes.

- **Red style:** These deep, dark, and powerful wines have a full and concentrated fruit flavor, with a soft, spicy aftertaste. Mourvèdre, and Syrah (no variety may exceed 90%), plus Cinsault and no more than 30% Carignan (Grenache Gris is no longer allowed)
- **White style:** These wines had to be sold as Vin de Pays de la Côte Vermeille until 2002, when white Collioure was first allowed. and/or Grenache Gris, plus a maximum of 30% in total (and individually a maximum of 15%) of Malvoisie, Macabeo, Marsanne, Rousanne, and Velmentino ROSÉ I have not…

#### CORBIÈRES AOC

When this appellation was elevated to full AOC status in December 1985, its area of production was practically halved to its current realm of 57,000 acres (23,000 hectares).

- **Red style:** These wines have an excellent color, a full, spicy-fruity nose, and a creamy-clean, soft palate that often hints of cherries, raspberries, and vanilla. Pelut, Mourvèdre, and Syrah, plus Carignan, Picpoul Noir, Terret, and a maximum of 20% Cinsault (Macabéo and Bourboulenc are no…
- **White style:** Soft, almost clinically clean, dry wines that have acquired a more aromatic character in recent vintages—Château Meunier St.-Louis is probably the best example—but many of the wines should be more expressive. There have been some successful experiments with oak fermentation.…
- **Grapes:** A minimum of 50% of Grenache, Lladoner; Bourboulenc, Grenache Blanc, Macabéo,
- **Aging:** 2–5 years (3–8 years in exceptional cases); 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Clos de L’Anhel, Des Chandelles, Clos

#### COSTIÈRES DE NÎMES AOC

Formerly a VDQS called Costières du Gard, these wines have never been special, but most have been better than many a lackluster wine from far more famous appellations, and in this respect Costières de Nîmes well deserved its promotion to AOC status in 1986.

- **Red style:** Simple, light, fruity wines are the norm, yet the best are round, aromatic, and spicy. Cinsault, a minimum of 25% Grenache, and at least 20% (in total or each) of Mourvèdre and Syrah (better cuvées)
- **White style:** Fresh, soft, but uninspiring. These wines may be sold from December 1 following the harvest without any mention of primeur or nouveau. (maximum 30%), Ugni Blanc, plus Macabéo, Marsanne, Roussanne, and Vermentino ROSÉ Good-value dry wine with a delightful color and ripe fruit.…

#### DE FRONTIGNAN AOC

- **White style:** The VDNs are delightful, goldencolored, raisiny-rich, sweet, and delicious wines that have a succulent, honeyed aftertaste with a somewhat fatter style than those of Beaumes, although many lack its finesse. The VDLs are much sweeter. la Peyrade

#### LIMOUX AOC

See Maury AOC Located in Le Petit Causse, the central-northern zone of Minervois, La Livinière was in 1999 the first cru in this appellation to be recognized in its own right.

- **Red style:** Newly introduced in 2004. Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon, Carignan, Grenache, malbec and Syrah. The Carignan must not exceed 10% and is due to be phased out by 2010. there must be at least three varieties, no two of which may exceed 90% of the total blend.

#### MAURY AOC

Despite the long list of possibilities, these fortified wines are mostly pure Grenache, a fact that is recognized by the gradual increase in the compulsory percentage of this grape—the minimum was just 50 percent 10 years ago.

#### MINERVOIS AOC

North of Corbières and adjoining the western extremity of the Coteaux du Languedoc, the rocky Minervois area has the typically hot and arid air of southern France.

- **Red style:** At worst, these wines are rough and ready for an AOC, but some of the best domaines also produce vins de pays that are better than the Minervois of other properties, thus their Minervois can be very good indeed. Pelut, Mourvèdre, and Syrah (these last two must represent at least…
- **White style:** Less than one percent of Minervois is white. A simple, dry, and fruity wine fermented at cooler temperatures than was once standard, it is now fresher and more aromatic. Marsanne, Roussanne, Vermentino, plus not more than 20% Clairette, Muscat à Petits Grains (itself limited to…
- **Grapes:** A minimum of 40% Grenache, Lladoner; Bourboulenc, Grenache Blanc, Macabéo,
- **Aging:** 1–5 years; Within 1 year

#### PIC-ST.-LOUP AOC

Covering six communes, this white-wine-only appellation must be made, as the name suggests, from 100 percent Picpoul, which in Pinet produces a lively young wine that quickly tires, and thus must be drunk as young as possible.

These sandy-soil vineyards just south of Narbonne overlap the Corbières appellation, but the wines, mostly red with some rosé, are rather stern and four-square, although the best can fill out and soften up with a few years in bottle.

#### ST.-SATURNIN AOC

Named after the first bishop of Toulouse, these red and rosé wines come from three communes in the foothills of the Cévennes Mountains, just west of Montpeyroux, where deep-colored, fine, and full-flavored red wines are possible, if not always evident.

- **Producers:** Lucian, C. V. de St-Saturnin

#### VÉRARGUES AOC

Red and rosé wines from 12 communes in Hérault and one in Gard, all in the vicinity of the pic, or peak, of Pic St.-Loup, including some high-altitude locations that must rank among the coolest vineyards in southern France.

- **Producers:** Devois du Claus (Elevé en Fûts de Chêne), Château du Grès-St.-Paul

Situated between Sartène and Porto Vecchio, Vin de Corse Figari is a subappellation of Vin de Corse AOC and requires the same grape varieties and technical level.


#### PROVENCE AOC

The red wine of this Provence appellation is a true vin de garde and deserves recognition. The white wine encépagement changed in 2003.

- **Red style:** Deep, dark, and rich, with creamy-spicy cherry and plum flavors. and Syrah, no two varieties of which must account for more than 90% of the entire blend, plus a maximum 30% in total of one or more of the following: Carignan, Cinsault and Counoise, with a maximum of 20% Cabernet…
- **Grapes:** A minimum of 60% Grenache, Mourvèdre; A minimum of 60% Cinsault, Grenache, and
- **Aging:** 4–10 years; 1–2 years
- **Producers:** Hauvette Y, Mas Sainte-Berthe (Louis

#### SARTENE AOC

South of Ajaccio, this subappellation of Vin de Corse AOC requires the same grape varieties and conforms to the same technical level.

- **Producers:** Clos d’Alzeto, Saparale

#### VECCHIO AOC

The southeastern edge of Corsica, around Porto Vecchio, is a subappellation of Vin de Corse AOC and requires the same grape varieties and technical level.

Friuli-Venezia Giulia See Friuli-Aquilea DOC. BARDOLINO SUPERIORE DOCG Veneto Bardolino DOC produces dry, sometimes frizzante, red and rosé (chiaretto) wines from the southeastern shores of Lake Garda.

- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Guerrieri-Rizzardi

#### ARBOREA DOC

Sardinia Three varietal wines: Sangiovese red, Sangiovese rosato, and a dry or semisweet Trebbiano white. ASPRINIO Basilicata This grape is always grown on its own rootstock and produces a green, often mean, dry white that is often frizzante.


#### ARNAD-MONTJOVAT DOC

Valle d’Aosta A red-only subappellation of the regional Valle d’Aosta DOC producing Nebbiolo-based red wines, with up to 30 percent Dolcetto, Freisa, Neyret, Pinot Noir, and Vien de Nus.


#### ARNEIS DI ROERO DOC

Piedmont These wines are produced from the ancient Arneis grape grown in the hills north of Alba. Formerly a vino da tavola, the high price and reputation of these wines ensured they would eventually become part of the Roero DOC.

- **Aging:** 3–5 years
- **Producers:** Ceretto (Blangé), Carlo Deltetto, Bruno

#### AROMATIZZATO DOC

Sicily See Marsala DOC.


#### ASOLANI DOC

Veneto From vineyards at the foot of the aptly named Mount Grappa, most of wines are varietals, comprising two reds (Merlot and Cabernet); four whites (Prosecco, Chardonnay, Pinot Bianco, and Pinot Grigio); and three spumante (Chardonnay, Pinot Bianco, and Prosecco).

- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Fernando Berta, Abbazia di Nervesa, Pravis (Le Frate), Reichsteiner

#### ASPRINIO DI AVERSA DOC

Campania Fresh, crisp, Vinho Verde-look-alike dry white wine from Asprinio grapes grown on vines trained to trees. Usually still, frizzantino, or frizzante, but sometimes fully sparkling.


#### ASTI DOCG

Piedmont Formerly sold as Asti Spumante, the spumante (which means “sparkling”) has been removed because it has become tarnished by cheap products that also use the term.

- **Aging:** nown simply as Asti and has been promoted; Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Barbero (Conte di Cavour), Walter Barbero

#### BARBARESCO DOCG

Piedmont Generally more feminine and elegant than Barolo, Barbaresco has a greater suppleness, softer fruit, and a more obvious charm, although some producers overlap the weightier Barolo style.

- **Aging:** 5–20 years
- **Producers:** Produttori di Barbaresco, Ceretto, Pio, Braida (Bricco dell’Uccellone), Castello di

#### BARBERA D’ALBA DOC

Piedmont Barbera is the most prolific Piedmont vine and, as such, has suffered unfairly from a somewhat lowly image.

- **Aging:** 5–12 years
- **Producers:** Marziano Abbona, Pio Cesare, Fratelli

#### BARBERA D’ASTI DOC

Piedmont Similar in character to Barbera d’Alba, but softer and more supple, with simpler generic wines, but equally profound single-vineyard wines.

- **Aging:** 3–8 years
- **Producers:** Marchesi Alfieri, Cascina la Barbatella, C. S. di Castagnole Monferrato (Barbera

#### BAROLO DOCG

Piedmont Barolo is unquestionably Italy’s greatest wine appellation and its finest wines are the ultimate expression of the Nebbiolo grape.


#### BAROLO CHINATO DOCG

Piedmont This Barolo, aromatized with quinine, is a DOCG, which is a bit like classifying Bordeaux’s aperitif “Lillet” as a cru classé.

- **Aging:** 8–25 years
- **Producers:** Marziano Abbona, Accomasso, Elio

#### BATTAGLIA DOC

Lombardy Dry, full-flavored white wine with a flowery aroma and slightly bitter aftertaste, and a sweet, liquoroso wine, made from the Tocai Friulano.


#### BIANCO DI CUSTOZA DOC

Veneto Effectively the white wines of Bardolino, they can be scented with a smooth aftertaste and may sometimes be sparkling.


#### BOCA DOC

Piedmont These are medium- to full-bodied, spicy red Nebbiolo wines. They are hard to find, but wines have been made here since Roman times and when found can be good value.

- **Aging:** 3–6 years
- **Producers:** Antonio Vallana, Podere ai Valloni

#### BOTTICINO DOC

Lombardy Full-bodied, Barbera-based red wines with a good level of alcohol and a light tannic structure. CABERNET-MERLOT Lombardy A relative newcomer to Lombardy, this classic Bordeaux mix has shown outstanding potential.

- **Aging:** 3–5 years
- **Producers:** Miro Bonetti, Benedetto Tognazzi, Bellavista (Solesine), Ca’del Bosco

#### BOZNER LEITEN DOC

Trentino-Alto Adige See Alto Adige DOC.


#### BRACHETTO D’ACQUI DOCG

Piedmont Sweet, frizzante (semi-sparkling), and sparkling red wines that are grapey and Muscat-like.


#### BRAMATERRA DOC

Piedmont Good value, full-bodied red wines produced primarily from Nebbiolo, but which may include Bonarda, Croatina, and Vespolina grapes.

- **Aging:** 3–6 years
- **Producers:** Luigi Perazzi, Fabrizio Sella

#### BREGANZE DOC

Veneto This is one of Italy’s unsung DOC heroes, largely due to the excellence of Maculan. As a generic red, Breganze is an excellent Merlotbased wine with the possible addition of Pinot Noir, Freisa, Marzemino, Gropello Gentile, Cabernet Franc, and Cabernet Sauvignon.

- **Aging:** 2–5 years (red), 1–2 years (white); 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Bartolomeo da Breganze, Maculan, Edi Kante

#### BRESCIANO DOC

Lombardy This area makes light, fruity, slightly bitter red wines that are no more interesting than the bulk of Valpolicella, produced on the opposite bank of Lake Garda.


#### BRINDISI DOC

Apulia


#### BUTTAFUOCO DOC

Lombardy See Oltrepò Pavese DOC.


#### CAGLIARI DOC

Sardinia Four varietals: Malvasia (a smooth, delicately scented red wine in dry, sweet, and liquoroso styles); Moscato (rich, succulently sweet white wines that may be fortified); Nasco (finely scented, delicate, dry and sweet white wines that can also be liquoroso); and Nuragus 

- **Aging:** 1–2 years
- **Producers:** Meloni O

#### CALUSO DOC

Piedmont


#### CALUSO PASSITO DOC

Piedmont Fragrant yet full-bodied, sweet white wines made from passito Erbaluce grapes.

- **Aging:** 3–5 years
- **Producers:** Vittorio Boratto

#### CAMPI FLEGREI DOC

Campania Two generic blends: red (Piedirosso, Aglianico, and Sciascinoso) and white (Falanghina, Biancolella, and Coda del Volpe); plus one redwine varietal: Piedirosso (dry or passito dry-tosweet); and one dry white varietal Falanghina (still or spumante).


#### CANTAVENNA DOC

Piedmont A full-bodied red wine blend of mostly Barbera, Grignolino, and sometimes Freisa. It is made by a cooperativa called Rubino, which was solely responsible for creating this appellation, but failed to interest other producers.

- **Producers:** Scarpa

#### CAPRI DOC

ALCAMO or BIANCO


#### CAPRIANO DEL COLLE DOC

Lombardy Rarely encountered red wines made from the Sangiovese, which are blended with Marzemino, Barbera, and Merlot, and tart white wines made from the Trebbiano.


#### CAREMA DOC

Piedmont Soft, medium-bodied Nebbiolo wines that are grown on the mountainous slopes close to the border with the Valle d’Aosta.

- **Aging:** 2–5 years
- **Producers:** Luigi Ferrando

#### CARIGNANO DEL SULCIS DOC

Sardinia Promising red and rosé wines from the Carignan grape, to which may be added a little Monica, Pascale, and Alicante Bouschet.

- **Aging:** 1–4 years
- **Producers:** CS di Santadi (Riserva Rocca Rubia, Terre

#### CARMIGNANO DOC

Tuscany Since Carmignano achieved DOCG status, the DOC has been used for rosato, vin santo, and rosé vin santo, called Occhio di Pernice (even though these styles were not allowed before the upgrading).


#### CARMIGNANO DOCG

Tuscany Only traditional red Carmignano from this tiny appellation west of Florence may claim DOCG status. Other wines are classified as either Carmignano DOC or Barco Reale di Carmignano DOC.

- **Aging:** 4–10 years
- **Producers:** Fattoria di Ambra, di Artimino, Fattoria, Tuscany Castello di Ama (Colline di Ama)

#### CARSO DOC

Friuli-Venezia Giulia Carso Terrano must consist of at least 85 percent Terrano (also known as Mondeuse or Refosco), which makes a deep, dark, and full red wine that can be fat and juicy.

- **Producers:** Azienda Agricola Inama (Campo dei Tovi)

#### CASTEL DEL MONTE DOC

Apulia The region’s best-known wine is named after the 13th-century castle built by Emperor Frederick von Hohenstaufen. With the exception of a red riserva made by Rivera and called II Falcone (The Falcon), the wines are less lofty than the name of this DOC suggests.


#### CASTEL SAN LORENZO DOC

Campania A relatively new DOC covering several communes in the province of Salerno, Castel San Lorenzo encompasses generic red and rosé (Barbera and Sangiovese), white (Trebbiano and Malvasia), one red wine varietal (Barbera) and one sweet white varietal (Moscato, still or spuman


#### CASTELLER DOC

Trentino-Alto Adige The thin strip of hilly vineyards that produces these wines is virtually identical to the Trentino DOC.

- **Aging:** 2–5 years (red), 1–2 years (white)
- **Producers:** Villa Sceriman O, Vignalta

#### CASTELLI DI JESI DOC

Marches These are popular, lean, fresh, and mostly uninteresting dry white wines. Spumante, frizzante, and frizzantino versions are also produced.

- **Aging:** 1–4 years
- **Producers:** Brunori, Bucci, Fazi Battaglia (Le Moie)

#### CELLATICA DOC

Piedmont Sweet, red, non-DOC Brachetto is often made by Asti producers. Lombardy This aromatic and flavorsome red wine has been made in the hills overlooking Brescia for 400 years.

- **Aging:** 2–6 years
- **Producers:** Batasiolo, Barbi

#### CENTRALE DOC

Tuscany The idea was fine, to provide an alternative appellation for lesser red, white, and rosé wines produced in the Chianti area and thus improve the DOCG through selection.


#### CERVETERI DOC

Latium Rustic Sangiovese-based reds, dry and semisweet Trebbiano-Malvasia white wines of decent, everyday quality.


#### CHAMBAVE DOC

Valle d’Aosta A subappellation of the regional Valle d’Aosta DOC, Chambave produces attractively scented, crisp red wines primarily from Petit Rouge grapes, plus up to 40 percent Dolcetto, Gamay, and Pinot Noir.

- **Aging:** 2–3 years (red and passito) • upon purchase
- **Producers:** La Crotta di Vegneron, Ezio Voyat, Angelo Gaja (Darmagi)

#### CILENTO DOC


#### CINQUETERRE DOC

Liguria These spectacular coastal vineyards produce good, though not exactly spectacular, delicately fruity dry white wines, and the somewhat more exciting Cinque Terre Sciacchetrà, which is a medium-sweet passito wine.

- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Forlini Cappellini

#### CIRÒ DOC


#### CLASSICO DOCG

Tuscany CASTELLI ROMANI Latium Castelli Romani are rarely exciting, dry and semisweet white, red, and rosé wines.


#### COLLI ALBANI DOC


#### COLLI ALTOTIBERINI DOC


#### COLLI AMERINI DOC

Umbria Dry whites from Trebbiano with the possible addition of Grechetto, Verdello, Garganega, and Malvasia, with red and rosé from Sangiovese plus the possibility of Montepulciano, Ciliegiolo, Canaiolo, Merlot, and Barbera.


#### COLLI BERICI DOC

Veneto In addition to a sparkling Garganega-based white wine, this DOC includes three red varietals (Merlot, Tocai Rosso, and Cabernet) and five white (Garganega, Tocai Bianco, Pinot Bianco, Sauvignon, and Chardonnay).

- **Aging:** 2–5 years (red), 1–2 years (white); 3–8 years (red), 1–3 years (white)
- **Producers:** Castello di Belvedere, Villa dal Ferro, Borgo del Tiglio, Girolamo Dorigo, Le Due

#### COLLI DEL TRASIMENO DOC

Umbria A very large DOC area on the Tuscan border. The dry and off-dry whites are ordinary, but the reds, in which the bitter edge of Sangiovese is softened with Gamay, Cilíegiolo, Malvasia, and Trebbiano are more interesting.

- **Aging:** 2–5 years
- **Producers:** La Fiorita

#### COLLI DI LUNI DOC

Liguria Sangiovese-based reds and Vermentino whites from the eastern extremity of Liguria, bordering and even overlapping part of Tuscany.

- **Aging:** 2–5 years (red) and 1–2 years (white)
- **Producers:** La Colombiera

#### COLLI EUGANEI DOC

Veneto Bordering Colli Berici to the southeast, this area produces soft, full-bodied, dry or semisweet red-wine blends (from Merlot plus Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon, Barbera, and Raboso) and four red varietals: Cabernet, Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon, and Merlot.

- **Aging:** 1–4 years (red), 1–3 years (white)
- **Producers:** Allegrini (Pelara, La Poja)

#### COLLI LANUVINI DOC

Latium Smooth, white wines, either dry or semisweet.


#### COLLI MARTANI DOC

Umbria This covers four varietal wines from a large but promising area encompassing the Montefalco DOC: Sangiovese, Trebbiano, Grechetto, and the single-commune Grechetto di Todi.


#### COLLI PERUGINI DOC

Umbria Dry, slightly fruity, Trebbiano-based white wines, full-bodied red wines, and dry, fresh rosé wines, primarily from Sangiovese grapes.


#### COLLI PIACENTINI DOC

Emilia-Romagna See Colli Piacentini DOC. VERDICCHIO DEI


#### COLLI TORTONESI DOC

Piedmont Robust and rather rustic, full-bodied Barbera reds, and crisp, dry, rather lightweight, and sometimes frizzante Cortese whites.


#### COLLINE LUCCHESI DOC

Latium Soft and fruity, dry and semisweet white wines that can be spumante. Tuscany This DOC produces light, soft, Chianti-like reds and bland, dry, Trebbiano-based whites.


#### COLLINE NOVARESI DOC

Piedmont A new DOC for red and white wines from the Novarese province northwest of Milan; the white is dry and made exclusively from the Erbaluce grape, whereas the red is a blend of at least 40 percent Uva Rara and 30 percent Nebbiolo, plus up to 30 percent Vespolina and Croatin


#### COLOMBANO DOC

Lombardy Rich, robust, if somewhat rustic red wines made from Croatina, Barbera, and Uva Rara are produced by this DOC. San Colombano is the only DOC in the Milan province.

- **Aging:** 2–5 years
- **Producers:** Carlo Pietrasanta

#### COLONNA DOC

Latium These dry or semisweet, Malvasia-based white wines may bear the name of one or both of the above towns on the label.


#### CONTESSA ENTELLINA DOC

Sicily


#### COPERTINO DOC

Apulia This appellation is named after the town of Copertino, although the wines that qualify for this DOC can also come from five other Campania This appellation celebrates Falernum, the wine so enjoyed in Ancient Rome, which was made in the northwest of Campania and Latium.

- **Aging:** 3–7 years (red) Upon purchase (white
- **Producers:** Villa Matilde, Michele Moio (Primitivo)

#### CORI DOC

Umbria An interesting DOC in the hilly upper Tiber Valley area, which produces dry white wines from Trebbiano and Malvasia, and firm, fruity reds from Sangiovese and Merlot.


#### CORTESE DI GAVI DOCG

DOLCETTO-NEBBIOLO FLÉTRI Piedmont When backed up by full, firm Nebbiolo, the soft, lush fruit of Dolcetto remains remarkably fresh for a considerable time in bottle.


#### D’AOSTE DOC

Valle d’Aosta A regional DOC encompassing 20 different styles of wine, Valle d’Aosta took under its wings the only two DOCs formerly recognized in the region: Donnaz and Enfer d’Arvier.

- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Grosjean, La Crotta di Vegneron

#### D’ARDA DOC

Emilia-Romagna See Colli Piacentini DOC.


#### D’ASTI DOC

Piedmont These are lightly aromatic, sweet, red, and rosé wines that may also be sparkling. MALVASIA DI CASTELNUOVO

- **Producers:** Bricco Mondalino

#### DEL FRIULI DOC

Friuli-Venezia Giulia CHARDONNAY Northeast Italy CABERNET FRANC Veneto Most Cabernet wines are DOC, but a few non-DOC exist and are invariably well worth seeking out as they tend to be barrique-aged selections of ripe, not too grassy fruit.

- **Aging:** 3–6 years
- **Producers:** Quintarelli (Alerzo), Anselmi (Realda), Le Vigne di San Pietro, Tr entino-Alto Adige Tenuta San Leonardo

#### DI BOLZANO DOC

Trentino-Alto Adige See Alto Adige DOC.


#### DI GAVI DOCG

Piedmont The Freisa is a black grape of refreshing acidity, which makes ruby-red wines with characteristic raspberry and rose-petal aromas for early drinking.

- **Aging:** 3–6 years (up to 10 for the very best)
- **Producers:** Clerico, Aldo Conterno, Coppo

#### DIANO D’ALBA DOC

See Dolcetto di Diano d’Alba DOC. NORTHWEST


#### DOGLIANI DOC

Piedmont Most Dolcetto from Dogliani used to be made to drink young and fruity, but more is now being made in a vin de garde style.

- **Producers:** Chionetti, Luigi Einaudi, Angelo Gaja

#### DOLCEACQUA DOC

Liguria This DOC produces light, easy-drinking red wines that are capable of rich, lush fruit, a soft texture, and spicy-aromatic aftertaste.

- **Aging:** 1–4 years
- **Producers:** Giobatta Cane, Lupi, Antonio Perrino

#### DOLCETTO D’ACQUI DOC

Piedmont Dolcetto is a plump grape with a low acid content that is traditionally used to make cheerful, Beaujolais-type wines that are deep purple in color and best enjoyed young.

- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Viticoltori dell’Acquese, Villa Banficut

#### DOLCETTO D’ALBA DOC

Piedmont Dolcetto d’Alba DOC produces some soft, smooth, juicy wines that should be drunk while they are young, fresh, and fruity.

- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Elio Altare, Azelia, Batasiolo, Fratelli, Cascina Drago (Bricco del Drago, especially

#### DOLCETTO D’ASTI DOC

Piedmont These wines are lighter than Dolcetto d’Alba. DOLCETTO DELLE LANGHE


#### DOLCETTO DI OVADA DOC

These are fresh, dry, light-bodied white wines made from the Erbaluce, which is a rather undistinguished grape. A far more interesting passito version exists and it is probably this that gave Erbaluce its overrated, albeit localized, reputation.

- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Luigi Ferrando

#### DON BOSCO DOC

Piedmont The DOC of Malvasia di Castelnuovo Don Bosco produces attractive, lightly aromatic, sweet, red, still, and sparkling wines.

- **Aging:** 1–2 years
- **Producers:** Bava

#### DONNICI DOC

Calabria Fruity, cherry-colored red and rosé wines (traditionally chiaretto) from Gaglioppo and Greco Nero grapes grown in the province of Cosenza.


#### EISACKTALER DOC

Trentino-Alto Adige See Alto Adige DOC.


#### ELBA DOC

Tuscany The range of wines from the vacation isle of Elba have been expanded to include 10 types: Trebbiano-based dry white; Sangiovese-based red, and riserva red; rosato; Ansonica dell’Elba (dry white from the Ansonica, better known as the Inzolia grape of Sicily); Ansonica Pass

- **Aging:** In situ only
- **Producers:** Acquabona

#### ELORO DOC

Campania Vines struggle in the rocky vineyards of Cilento, where generic red and rosé are made from Aglianico, Piedirosso, and Barbera, and generic white from Fiano, Trebbiano, Greco, and Malvasia.


#### ETNA DOC

Calabria Strong, alcoholic Gaglioppo-based red and rosé, and Greco-based dry white wines that rely too heavily on the Cirò name, which was famous in ancient times.

- **Producers:** Benanti (Pietramarina)

#### ETSCHTALER DOC

Trentino-Alto Adige and Veneto This huge generic denomination encompasses both the Alto Adige and Trentino DOCs, a myriad of subappellations, and extends well into the Veneto.

- **Aging:** Upon purchase for most, 2–5 years for; 6–15 years
- **Producers:** Valpolicella Allegrini (La Grola), Bolla, Allegrini (Palazzo alla Torre), Boscaini (Le

#### FALERNO DEL MASSICO DOC

This new DOC encompasses vineyards in the commune of Contessa Entellina in the province of Palermo for a delicate dry white blend of Inzolia, Catarratto, Garganega, Chardonnay, Sauvignon, and Müller-Thurgau; and three dry white-wine varietals, Chardonnay, Grecanico (Garganega), a


#### FARA DOC

Piedmont In the hands of someone like Dessilani, Fara DOC proves to be an underrated, enjoyable, Nebbiolo-based wine with lots of fruit combined with a spicy-scented character.

- **Aging:** 3–6 years (up to 10 for the very best)
- **Producers:** Luigi Dessilani (Caramino), Cascina Scarsi Olivi

#### FARO DOC

Sicily Ruby-colored, medium-bodied but firmly flavored red wines from Nerello and Nocera grapes grown around Messina. Calabrese, Gaglioppo, and Sangiovese may also be used.

- **Aging:** 2–5 years
- **Producers:** Bagni, Palari

#### FIANO DI AVELLINO DOCG

Campania Mastroberardino’s Vignadora is the best wine in this unusual, but well above average, dry white wine DOCG, made from Fiano grapes grown in the hilly hinterland of Avellino.

- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Mastroberardino

#### FRANCA DOC

Apulia Dark-skinned Malvasia is commonly found in the Salento district, and traditionally complements the local Negroamaro grape, although it is sometimes made into a pure varietal wine, both as DOC and vini da tavola.


#### FRANCIACORTA DOCG


#### FRANCIACORTA DOC

Lombardy Part of the original Franciacorta appellation since 1967, the still wines were renamed Terre di Franciacorta in 1995 when the name in its singular form, Terra di Franciacorta, was reserved for the new DOCG.

- **Aging:** 3–8 years (red), 1–3 years (white)
- **Producers:** Ca’del Bosco, Enrico Gatti, Ragnoli

#### FRASCATI DOC

Latium Most Frascati used to be flabby or oxidized, but with recent improvements in vinification techniques they are now invariably fresh and clean, although many still have a bland, peardrop aroma and taste.

- **Aging:** 1–2 years
- **Producers:** Colli di Catone (especially Colle Gaio—, Bigi (Marrano)

#### FREISA D’ASTI DOC

Piedmont Known for centuries, and a favorite of King Victor Emmanuel, this is the original and most famous Freisa. These fruity red wines may be made fully sparkling, frizzante, or still, and in both dry and sweet styles.

- **Aging:** 3–6 years (up to 10 for the very best)
- **Producers:** Gilli

#### FREISA DI CHIERI DOC

Piedmont Freisa di Chieri produces the same styles of wine as Freisa d’Asti from just outside Turin.


#### FRIULI-AQUILEA DOC

Friuli-Venezia Giulia Formerly know simply as Aquilea, this is a wide-ranging appellation that covers five varietal red wines (Merlot, Cabernet, Cabernet Franc, Cabernet Sauvignon, and Refosco); eight white varietals (Chardonnay, Tocai Friulano, Pinot Bianco, Pinot Grigio, Riesli

- **Aging:** 1–4 years (red), 1–3 years (white, rosé, and
- **Producers:** Zonin (Tenuta Cà Bolani)

#### FRIULI-GRAVE DOC

Friuli-Venezia Giulia Formerly called Grave del Friuli, this massive appellation spreads out on both sides of the Tagliamento River between Sacile in the west and Cividale di Friuli in the east, and accounts for over half of the region’s total production.

- **Aging:** 1–4 years (red), 1–3 years (white, rosé, and
- **Producers:** Borgo Magredo, Pighin, Vigneti le Monde

#### GABIANO DOC


#### GALLURA DOCG

Sardinia Despite cool fermentation, this is the best Vermentino Sardinia offers, the hilly terroir of Gallura producing intense flavors.

- **Aging:** 1–2 years
- **Producers:** Tenuta di Capichera, C. S. di Gallura

#### GAMBELLARA DOC

Veneto Produces scented, dry, or sometimes semisweet white wines and fruity, semisweet, still, frizzante or fully sparkling white recioto.

- **Aging:** 2–5 years
- **Producers:** La Biancara

#### GARDA DOC

Lombardy Although vines have been cultivated since ancient times in Lake Garda, today viticulture is one of the least important crops.


#### GATTINARA DOCG

Piedmont From Nebbiolo and up to 10 percent Bonarda grown on the right bank of the Sesia River in northern Piedmont, Gattinara can be a fine, though not a great, wine, especially now that overcropping problems have been reduced since Gattinara attained DOCG status.

- **Aging:** 6–15 years; 2–3 years
- **Producers:** Mario Antoniolo, Le Colline, Travaglini, Nicola Bergaglio, Chiarlo (Fior di Rovere)

#### GHEMME DOCG

Piedmont A Nebbiolo-based wine produced on the bank opposite Gattinara, Ghemme is usually seen as the inferior of the two appellations, but it has never been as persistently overcropped and is generally more consistent.

- **Aging:** 4–15 years
- **Producers:** Antichi Vigneti di Cantelupo, Le Colline

#### GIMIGNANO DOCG

Tuscany Cabernet Sauvignon was originally blended with Sangiovese to supply fruit and accessibility within a classic fine-wine structure.

- **Aging:** 1–3 years
- **Producers:** Altesino (Alte d’Altesi), Antinori, Falchini, Panizzi, Teruzzi & Puthod

#### GIOIA DEL COLLE DOC

Apulia For this large DOC in the province of Bari there are generic red and rosé blends (Primitivo, Montepulciano, Sangiovese, Negroamaro, and Malvasia Nera); a dry white (Trebbiano and anything else that moves); and two varietals, a sweetish Primitivo and an intensely sweet Alea


#### GIRO DI CAGLIARI DOC

Sardinia Smooth, alcoholic, dry and sweet red wines and dry and sweet fortified wines.


#### GORIZIANO DOC

Friuli-Venezia Giulia COLLI DI BOLZANO or


#### GRAGNANO DOC

Campania See Penisola Sorrentina DOC.


#### GRAVE DEL FRIULI DOC

Friuli-Venezia Giulia See Friuli-Grave DOC. ISONZO or ISONZO


#### GRAVINA DOC

Apulia Dry or semisweet, blended white wines that may be still or spumante, made from Malvasia, Greco, and Bianco d’Alessano, with the possible addition of Bombino, Trebbiano, and Verdeca.


#### GRECO DI BIANCO DOC

Calabria Made on the very tip of Calabria, around Bianco, from the Grechetto grape, known locally as Greco Bianco. With the exception of Umberto Ceratti’s exceptional rendition, Greco di Bianco is a simple passito wine.

- **Aging:** 3–5 years
- **Producers:** Umberto Ceratti

#### GRECO DI TUFO DOCG

Campania Delicate, dry, and soft white wines that may sometimes be spumante, from the true Greco grown north of Avellino.

- **Aging:** Upon purchase
- **Producers:** Mastroberardino (Vignadangelo)

#### GRIGNOLINO D’ASTI DOC

Lombardy The sparkling white and rosé wines made by Franciacorta were promoted to DOCG status in September 1995.

- **Aging:** 2–5 years
- **Producers:** Banfi, Bellavista, Fratelli Berlucchi

#### GRUMELLO DOC

Lombardy See Valtellina Superiore DOC.


#### GUARDIOLO DOC

#### HÜGEL DOC

Lombardy See Valtellina Superiore DOC. LAMBRUSCO


#### ISARCO DOC

Trentino-Alto Adige See Alto Adige DOC.

- **Producers:** D’Ambra

#### LANGHE DOC

Piedmont This area overlaps the DOCs Barbaresco, Barolo, Nebbiolo d’Alba, Dolcetto d’Alba, and Barbera d’Alba and, if used sensibly, through selection, could ensure the highest quality in those wines.

#### LETTERE DOC

Campania See Penisola Sorrentina DOC.

Apulia Negroamaro-based red and rosé, still or frizzante generic wines, which may be boosted by the addition of Montepulciano, Sangiovese, Bombino Nero, Pinot Noir, and Malvasia Nera.

#### LOCOROTONDO DOC

Apulia Fresh, lightly fruity, dry white wines of improving quality from Verdeca and Bianco d’Alessano, with the possible addition of Fiano, Malvasia, and Bombino, made in still and spumante styles.

- **Producers:** C. S. di Locorotondo

#### LUCERA DOC

Apulia The name of this full-bodied red uvaggio, which is made from seven grape varieties, loosely


#### LUGANA DOC

Lombardy These are soft, smooth, dry white wines made from Trebbiano grown on the shores of Lake Garda, slightly overlapping Veneto in northeast Italy.

- **Producers:** Cà dei Frati, Visconti, La Muiraghina

#### MAGDALENER DOC

Trentino-Alto Adige See Alto Adige DOC. SCHIAVA Trentino-Alto Adige This is the most widely cultivated variety in the Alto Adige, where it accounts for 60 percent of the vines grown.

Sardinia Rich, sweet and dry white wines, and sweet and dry liquoroso wines. Generally fuller-bodied than Malvasia di Cagliari.


#### MALVASIA DI CAGLIARI DOC

Sardinia Rich, sweet or dry, white wines, and dry or sweet, liquoroso wines. They are lighter but finer and more elegant wines than Malvasia di Bosa and traditionally are drier.

- **Producers:** Cantina de Bartoli, Florio, Carlo

#### MANDROLISAI DOC


#### MANDURIA DOC

Apulia Dry to semisweet, full-bodied red wines which may also be fortified and naturally sweet, and fortified and dry. Until I tasted Giordano’s 1993 vintage in 1996, no Primitivo had shown any class.

#### MANTOVANO DOC

- **Producers:** C. S. di Quistello

- **Producers:** Colle Picchioni (Oro), Villa Banfi (Mandrielle), Tenuta

Tuscany These are red, white, rosé, novello, and vin santo from the northern part of the province of Grosseto. A dry white Vermentino varietal wine and a pink passito called Occhio di Pernice are also included within Monteregio di Massa Marittima DOC.


#### MARSALA DOC

Sicily This wine was conceived by John Woodhouse, who began shipping it in 1773. Its name is Arabic, deriving from Marsah-el-Allah, the old name of the port of Marsala.

- **Producers:** Basilicata Fratelli d’Angelo, Paternosta

#### MATELICA DOC

Marches Made in a hilly area in the center of Macerata, these wines are slightly fatter than those of Castelli di Jesi, but most are just as uninteresting to drink.

- **Producers:** Fratelli Bisci, La Monacesca

#### MATINO DOC

Apulia Dry, robust red and slightly vinous rosé wines made from Negroamaro with the possible addition of Malvasia Nera.


#### MELISSA DOC

Sardinia Red and rosé wines with a bitter aftertaste blended from Bovale (Monastrell), Cannonau (Grenache), and Monica.


#### MONFERRATO DOC

Piedmont This is a relatively new DOC, which was introduced in 1995 for red, white, and ciaret or chiaretto (rosé) Dolcetto, Casalese, and Freisa from a wide area overlapping the Asti region.

- **Producers:** La Barbatella, Martinetti, Villa Sparina

#### MONFERRATO CASALESE DOC


#### MONREGALESI DOC

Piedmont Rare wines produced in tiny quantities, reputed to have an exceptionally fine aroma.

#### MONTALCINO DOC

Tuscany An ancient style of aromatic, sweet Muscat that was famous long before Brunello. Fortified versions and sweet frizzante are also possible.

#### MONTEFALCO DOCG

Umbria There is a significant difference in quality between these basic red and white wines and the more interesting, characterful DOCG Sagrantino of Montefalco.


#### MONTEFIASCONE DOC

Latium The name is the most memorable thing about these dry or semisweet white wines made from Trebbiano and Malvasia grapes grown around Lake Bolsena, adjacent to the Orvieto district.

#### MONTEPULCIANO DOCG

- **Producers:** Avignonesi, Bindella, Podere Boscarelli

#### MONTESCUDAIO DOC

Tuscany A Trebbiano-based dry white wine, a soft, slightly fruity, Sangiovese-based red, and a vin santo from the Cecina Valley.

#### MONTUNI DEL RENO DOC

Emilia-Romagna Light, dry or sweet, still or sparkling white wine with a slightly bitter finish. PAGADEBIT DI

#### MOSCATO DI CAGLIARI DOC

Sardinia See Cagliari DOC.


#### MOSCATO DI NOTO DOC

Sicily Still, spumante, or fortified semisweet and sweet Moscato wines.


#### MOSCATO DI SIRACUSA DOC

Sicily These sweet, smooth white wines are made from semi-passito grapes.


#### MOSCATO DI SORSOSENNORI DOC

Sardinia Sweet, rich, white Moscato and sweet, aromatic liquoroso. The wines are fuller than either Moscato di Cagliari or Moscato di Sardegna.


#### MOSCATO DI TRANI DOC

Apulia Tiny production of luscious, ultrasmooth, sweet white Moscato wines, which may be fortified. These wines are high quality and distinctive.

#### NARDO DOC

#### NURAGUS DI CAGLIARI DOC

Sardinia See Cagliari DOC.


#### NUS DOC

- **Producers:** Frescobaldi, Fattoria Petrognano

Liguria Four former vini da tavola are grouped together under this appellation, which covers the western Riviera of Liguria.

#### RAMANDOLO DOCG

Friuli-Venezia Giulia Sweet Verduzzo dessert wine. RECIOTO BIANCO DI CAMPOCIESA Veneto Soft, sweet, well-scented, golden-colored, nonDOC white wines made from passito grapes.

Marches See Colli Pesaresi DOC.

Apulia Dry, vinous red and rosé wines made from Montepulciano and Sangiovese, and dry, fresh whites from Bombino and Trebbiano, with the possible addition of Malvasia and Verdeca.
